# UXR FINDINGS REGISTER — Product Experience Certification Audit (2026-07-21)

> **Normative annex of the Master Roadmap §5.6 PROGRAM UXR** (`AKSHARA_CONSTITUTION_ALIGNED_MASTER_ROADMAP.md`).
> This register carries the **complete, verbatim finding-level evidence** from the 17-reviewer Product Experience
> Certification Audit so future implementation needs **no re-investigation**. Work items in PROGRAM UXR trace here
> by `F-id`. Rows may not be deleted/merged/re-scoped except under the roadmap's APPENDIX C change-control law.
>
> **Source of truth chain:** reconciled audit report → `docs/audits/PRODUCT_EXPERIENCE_CERTIFICATION_AUDIT_2026-07-21.md`;
> raw reviewer outputs → session workflow `wf_bee4a4f0-868` (journal preserved in the session transcript dir).
> **Evidence caveat:** all `file:line` references are against the working tree on `feature/qie-question-planning-layer`
> at audit time (2026-07-20/21). Re-verify locations on the converged trunk before fixing — the defect logic, not the
> line number, is the durable part (same law as PROGRAM ICA).

**Volume:** 250 findings from 17 reviewers · 56 raw P0 claims → **45 taken through adversarial verification** (cross-reviewer dedup + a verification cap of 45) → 15 confirmed P0 · 29 downgraded · 1 refuted. **The 11 P0 claims past the cap** (F-210…F-214, F-225…F-228, F-239, F-240) were never independently verified: 7 are duplicates/variants of verified clusters (web read-only, parent payment, PTM tile), and 4 (F-225, F-226, F-227 — library counter & lead creation; F-228 — web front-office, folds into the web cluster) carry their reviewer severity with an explicit **`unverified`** marker in PROGRAM UXR and must be re-verified at fix time.

**Severity semantics:** the *reviewer* severity is preserved on each finding; where an adversarial verifier re-graded a P0 claim, the **verified severity wins** and is shown on the finding (`verified:`). P0 = blocks/damages a core daily workflow or would embarrass in a sales demo; P1 = significant friction on a real workflow (fix before the affected surface goes live/scales); P2 = polish / missed opportunity.

---

## PART 1 — ADVERSARIALLY VERIFIED P0 CLAIMS (all 45, with verifier reasoning)

*Every P0-severity claim was independently re-examined by a refuter agent instructed to disprove it. The verdict and the*
*verifier's full reasoning are preserved — they contain mitigations, aggravations, and scope corrections that implementers*
*must read before acting. REFUTED / downgraded entries are recorded so they are never re-raised at the wrong severity.*

### V-01 · CONFIRMED **P1** — Parent 'PTM' nav tile dead-ends on an Access Denied screen
- **Module:** parent / navigation · **Platform:** all-mobile · **Role:** parent · **Reported by:** principal-product-designer
- **Original evidence:** lib/features/parent/shell/parent_shell.dart:95-96 puts route RouteNames.parentPtm ('/parent/ptm', route_names.dart:33) in the More sheet; lib/shared/navigation/persona_nav.dart:197-216 renders spec.more with NO SchoolBuildScope filter; '/parent/ptm' is in hiddenRoutePrefixes (lib/core/config/school_build_scope.dart:76-77); lib/router/app_router.dart:2610-2614 returns AccessDeniedScreen for it. Trace: parent taps More → PTM tile → Access Denied.
- **Original impact:** A first-class tile in the parent app's own navigation leads to a refusal screen. Parents are the largest user population; this looks broken in every demo and erodes trust in the whole More sheet.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Filter MoreNavDestination entries through SchoolBuildScope.isRouteHidden (and the backend-surface gate) inside MoreNavSheet/PersonaNavSpec so gated features never render an entry point; re-add the tile when the PTM backend ships.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The defect is genuine and exactly as traced — the nav layer failed to apply the SchoolBuildScope gate that the router applies, so an intentionally-hidden backendless feature is advertised as a first-class tile and dead-ends on a refusal screen. It also violates the project's own stated pattern (route_guards.dart P0-CODE-2 comment: backend-less surfaces must be HIDDEN, mirroring nav filtering). However, P0 (production-blocking) is inflated: no data loss, no security exposure, no core parent task is blocked (attendance, fees, exams, notices, transport, leave all work), and the fix is a one-line tile removal or a nav-side isRouteHidden filter. It is a high-visibility trust/polish defect affecting the largest user population and would appear in every demo — that is a strong P1 (fix before pilot/demo), not a release-blocking P0.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** Every citation checks out. (1) lib/features/parent/shell/parent_shell.dart:94-98 — MoreNavDestination(route: RouteNames.parentPtm, label: 'PTM') is in the parent More list. (2) lib/router/route_names.dart:33 — parentPtm = '/parent/ptm'. (3) lib/shared/navigation/persona_nav.dart:197-216 — MoreNavSheet renders `for (final d in spec.more)` with no filtering; grep confirms zero SchoolBuildScope references anywhere in lib/shared/navigation/ or lib/features/parent/. (4) lib/core/config/school_build_scope.dart:19 — enabled = true; line 77 — RouteNames.parentPtm is in hiddenRoutePrefixes (comment: 'Gated OFF until the PTM backend ships — mock-only, lost edits on restart, CORE-1/PAR-4'). (5) lib/router/app_router.dart:2610-2619 — parentPtmRouteBuilder returns const AccessDeniedScreen() when isRouteHidden. Mitigation search: the ErpRouteGuard filter (route_guards.dart:411) only covers admin ERP routes, not the parent shell; no redirect intercepts '/parent/ptm'; no alternate PTM surface exists for parents (parentMeetings '/parent-meetings' is gated off by the same list). The trace parent → More → PTM → Access Denied is real in the shipped school build.

### V-02 · CONFIRMED **P1** — Exams module is invisible in the Admin Hub and mobile admin bottom nav — no workspace contains AdminModule.exams
- **Module:** exams / admin navigation · **Platform:** all · **Role:** principal, schoolAdmin, exam coordinator · **Reported by:** senior-ux-researcher
- **Original evidence:** lib/core/workspace/workspace.dart:67-85 — the schoolAdministration workspace's modules set lists 16 modules but omits AdminModule.exams (no other workspace contains it either). lib/features/admin/admin_navigation_provider.dart:272-284 — workspaceScopedNavDestinationsProvider filters nav to workspace.containsModule(). lib/core/workspace/workspace_providers.dart:24-34 — activeWorkspaceProvider always falls back to the user's first workspace, so the filter is always active for staff. Consumers: lib/features/admin/screens/admin_hub_screen.dart:28 (hub card grid, all form factors) and lib/features/admin/admin_bottom_nav.dart:47 (phone-only bottom nav). The Exams destination itself exists with an 'A5 — un-bury the flagship Exam Administration module' comment (admin_navigation_provider.dart:58-68) but only the desktop/tablet rail uses the unscoped provider (admin_navigation_rail.dart:56). Only remaining mobile entry points: lib/features/management/academics/management_academics_screen.dart:119 and lib/features/school_completion/school_completion_hub_screen.dart:25 — in-page shortcuts.
- **Original impact:** On a mobile-first product whose stated top-priority module is Exams, a principal on a phone cannot find Exams in the bottom nav or the Admin Hub ('Jump to a module you are authorized to access' — and Exams is not there). On desktop the rail shows Exams while the hub grid on the same screen does not — contradictory same-screen navigation. This re-creates the exact burial the A5 fix documented, and would derail a sales demo of the flagship module.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Add AdminModule.exams to the schoolAdministration workspace modules set (one line in workspace.dart), and add a regression test asserting every destination in kAllAdminNavDestinations that survives school-build filtering belongs to at least one workspace.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The structural defect is real: AdminModule.exams is absent from every workspace's module set, which excludes the flagship Exams module from the workspace-scoped surfaces (Admin Hub card grid on all form factors, phone bottom-nav primary tabs) while the unscoped rail/drawer show it — a genuine same-screen inconsistency that contradicts the documented A5 "un-bury Exams" intent and is a one-line fix. But the P0 framing rests on "Exams is invisible / cannot be found on mobile," which does not survive contact with the code: the phone's standard More→drawer path (the designated route for all modules past the 4th tab, used equally by Alumni, Inventory, etc.) lists Exams as a labeled first-class destination, and desktop/tablet users see it in the rail at all times. Every role has a working, discoverable entry point on every form factor, so nothing is blocked for a real user or a demo — it is a discoverability/consistency defect on the flagship module, not a production blocker. Confirmed at P1: should be fixed before GA (add AdminModule.exams to the schoolAdministration set and reconcile the drawer's scoped-vs-unscoped inconsistency), but not release-blocking.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** All cited code is accurate: workspace.dart:67-85 omits AdminModule.exams from schoolAdministration (no other workspace has it); workspaceScopedNavDestinationsProvider (admin_navigation_provider.dart:272-284) filters to workspace.containsModule; activeWorkspaceProvider (workspace_providers.dart:24-34) defaults to first workspace so the filter is always active for principal/schoolAdmin; admin_hub_screen.dart:28 and admin_bottom_nav.dart:47 consume the scoped provider, so the Exams card is missing from the hub grid on all form factors and can never be a primary bottom-nav tab; the rail (admin_navigation_rail.dart:56) uses the unscoped provider so desktop shows Exams in the rail but not the hub grid. HOWEVER the reviewer missed a mitigation: the bottom nav's "More" tab and the hub's menu button open the drawer (admin_shell.dart:72 onMore: _openDrawer), which is AdminNavigationRail(inDrawer:true) reading the UNSCOPED adminNavDestinationsProvider — so Exams IS listed in the mobile module drawer as a first-class tile, one tap away, and is not hidden by SchoolBuildScope or SchoolCapabilityRegistry. The claim "a principal on a phone cannot find Exams" is false.

### V-03 · CONFIRMED **P1** — Production login copy leaks internal jargon: 'staging server' and 'demo-school phone number' shown to real parents
- **Module:** auth · **Platform:** all-mobile · **Role:** parent (first screen of every session) · **Reported by:** senior-ux-researcher
- **Original evidence:** lib/features/auth/login_screen.dart:156-159 — in the NON-demo branch the subtitle reads 'Enter your registered mobile number. OTP is verified by the staging server.' login_screen.dart:73-79 — the OTP-send failure SnackBar in the NON-demo branch reads 'Unable to send OTP. Use a registered demo-school phone number.' (the branches appear inverted: the demo branch gets the generic message).
- **Original impact:** The very first screen a parent — or a prospect in a sales demo — sees announces a 'staging server'; a failed OTP tells a real production user their school is a 'demo-school'. Erodes trust instantly and reads as an unfinished product. Copy-only fix, but P0 by demo-embarrassment on the entry screen.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Swap the two error strings and rewrite the production subtitle ('We'll send a one-time password to your registered mobile number'). Add a copy-lint rule banning 'staging', 'demo', 'mock', 'gap WEB-' in non-QA user-facing strings.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The evidence fully survives contact with the code — no misreading, and the supposed mitigation (release guard) actually guarantees the defect in production. However, P0 is inflated: it is a copy-only defect with zero functional, data, or security impact; login works and no user task is blocked. The misleading 'demo-school' failure message and 'staging server' subtitle are high-visibility trust/support issues on the entry screen — a must-fix before any demo or GA — which is P1 by a production-blocking P0 bar.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** Both cited strings exist verbatim at the cited lines and are in the NON-demo branches exactly as claimed: login_screen.dart:157-159 shows 'OTP is verified by the staging server.' when demoAuth==false, and :76-78 shows 'Unable to send OTP. Use a registered demo-school phone number.' when demoAuth==false (branches inverted vs. intent). Worse for refutation: environment.dart guardForRelease (:141-162) force-sets disableDemoAuth=true in every release binary, so isDemoAuthEnabledProvider (environment_provider.dart:11-13) is guaranteed false in production — the leaked copy is deterministically what real parents see. This LoginScreen is the routed parent login (app_router.dart:173). No golden screenshot of the login screen exists to cross-check, but the code path is unambiguous.

### V-04 · CONFIRMED **P0** — AI FAB is positioned directly over the middle bottom-nav tab, hijacking taps on Teach/Fees/Schedule
- **Module:** navigation / copilot · **Platform:** all-mobile · **Role:** teacher, parent, student · **Reported by:** mobile-ux-specialist
- **Original evidence:** lib/features/copilot/widgets/copilot_bottom_nav_ai_slot.dart:22-32 (Positioned left:0,right:0,top:-22, centered 56px button) painted last in the Stack over the NavigationBar (lib/shared/navigation/persona_nav.dart:86-119). With 5 slots (4 primary + More), the FAB sits exactly over slot 3: teacher 'Teach' = homework+exams (lib/features/teacher/shell/teacher_shell.dart:37-43), parent 'Fees' (lib/features/parent/shell/parent_shell.dart:47-48), student 'Schedule' (lib/features/student_app/shell/student_shell.dart:37-38). Default AI mode for these roles is bottomNavCenter (lib/features/copilot/settings/ai_access_preferences_provider.dart:50-54). Goldens test/golden/goldens/persona_shell_teacher_390x844.png and persona_shell_parent_390x844.png show the circular FAB covering the middle tab's icon with the orphaned label beneath it. At 390px width each slot is 78px; the 56px FAB (x 167-223) fully covers the middle tab's icon zone (icon ~x 183-207).
- **Original impact:** Tapping the icon of the Fees tab (parent), Teach tab (teacher, gateway to Exams — the top-priority module) or Schedule tab (student) opens the AI sheet instead of navigating. The tab is reachable only via the thin label strip below the FAB. Daily mis-taps on the highest-value destinations; visually the FAB appears mislabeled with the covered tab's label ('Teach'/'Fees'), which would embarrass in a sales demo.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Reserve the center slot for the AI entry (4 destinations become 2+2 around a dedicated center slot), or move the AI entry to the app bar for these personas. Never place a floating button over a live NavigationDestination.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The core claim survives contact with the code completely: the AI FAB is overlaid on a real, populated middle nav slot (the code comment calls it a 'center slot' but PersonaBottomNav never reserves one — all 5 slots carry real destinations), it is painted last and hit-tested first, and it is enabled by default for teacher/parent/student AND for any 'auto' user on mobile. The golden screenshot independently confirms the rendered overlap. Refutation attempts failed: no IgnorePointer/translucent hit behavior, no test coverage proving the middle tab is tappable, no alternate center-slot spec for bottomNavCenter mode. Severity is honest as P0 for this product: it is default-on for all three primary mobile personas on every screen, hijacks the icon-tap of the parent 'Fees' tab (the money/payment path), the teacher 'Teach' tab (gateway to Exams, the declared top-priority module), and visually mislabels the AI button with the covered tab's label — a guaranteed daily mis-tap and an immediate sales-demo embarrassment for a product whose north star is 'easiest mobile-first ERP'. The partial mitigation (thin label strip below the FAB still navigates; dashboard cards reach the same screens) keeps it from being a hard dead-end but does not lift it out of production-blocking territory, since the defect ships in the default configuration to every mobile user and the reviewer's impact statement already acknowledged the label-strip workaround accurately.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** Every cited location checks out. (1) lib/features/copilot/widgets/copilot_bottom_nav_ai_slot.dart:22-33: Positioned(left:0, right:0, top:-22) > Center > CopilotAiEntryButton(fabStyle:true); button default size is 56 (copilot_ai_entry_button.dart:22, width/height at :54-55) with its own Material+InkWell and no IgnorePointer anywhere, so it absorbs taps. (2) persona_nav.dart:86-119: the slot is the LAST child of the Stack after NavigationBar, so Flutter hit-tests it first; the Center defers-to-child, meaning only the 56px circle intercepts — but that circle overlaps the icon zone of the middle destination. (3) All three shells define exactly 4 primary tabs + More = 5 slots with the claimed middle (index 2) destination: teacher 'Teach' (homework + matchPrefix exams, teacher_shell.dart:37-43), parent 'Fees' (+payment/receipts prefixes, parent_shell.dart:46-55), student 'Schedule' (student_shell.dart:36-41). (4) ai_access_preferences_provider.dart:50-54 defaults parent/teacher/student to bottomNavCenter; worse, ai_access_mode.dart:29-33 resolves even 'auto' to bottomNavCenter on mobile, so the overlay is on for effectively every mobile persona user by default. (5) Rendered golden test/golden/goldens/persona_shell_teacher_390x844.png visually confirms: 5 tab slots, the circular FAB sits directly on top of the middle tab's icon with the orphaned label strip beneath it — it reads as if the FAB is the 'Teach' tab. Geometry math is correct (78px slots at 390px; FAB x 167-223 fully covers the middle icon ~x 183-207). (6) test/shared/navigation/persona_bottom_nav_test.dart has no test tapping the middle tab, so nothing masks or contradicts the defect. Mitigations I found: taps on the label strip below the circle and on slot edges still reach the NavigationBar's InkWell (the tab is not fully unreachable), the destinations are also reachable from dashboard cards, and users can manually switch AI mode in settings — none of which fixes the default experience.

### V-05 · CONFIRMED **P0** — Live-reachable parent 'Pay Now' completes a simulated payment with success screen and receipt
- **Module:** parent / fees / payment · **Platform:** all-mobile · **Role:** parent · **Reported by:** mobile-ux-specialist
- **Original evidence:** lib/features/parent/payment/parent_payment_provider.dart:104-163 — production flow is submitMockPayment(): initiate then confirm with a client-generated transactionRef 'txn_${DateTime.now().millisecondsSinceEpoch}', no gateway checkout UI (no UPI handoff, no card entry), then PaymentFlowPhase.success with receipt. Route /parent/payment is registered (lib/router/app_router.dart:371-377), reached from Pay Now (lib/router/parent_navigation.dart:115-118), and is NOT in the backend-less surface gate (lib/router/surface_backend_gate.dart:22-40), so it is reachable in a live API build. Backend has a real Razorpay layer awaiting credentials (supabase/functions/_shared/payment/payment_handlers.ts:199-210), which the client flow bypasses.
- **Original impact:** A parent selects a payment method, watches 'Processing payment', and receives a success screen and receipt without any money moving. This is a trust-destroying experience for the single most sensitive parent journey, and a school demo of fee collection would show fake receipts.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Gate /parent/payment behind the payment-gateway API flag exactly like the eight backend-less surfaces (show fees, receipts, and dues, but replace Pay Now with 'Pay at school office' until the gateway ships), and delete the client-generated transaction reference path.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The finding is fully confirmed and, if anything, understated: the reviewer framed it as a fake success screen, but the stub-mode backend also posts a genuine finance collection/receipt and clears the invoice, so a live parent tapping Pay Now would extinguish real dues without paying — a money-integrity defect on the single most sensitive parent journey, not just a trust/UX defect. No mitigations exist: the route is live-reachable (not in the backend-less surface gate, Pay Now wired from the fees screen for the parent role), there is no simulation/sandbox disclosure anywhere in the payment UI, and no alternate real payment path exists in the client. The only softening context is that this maps to the already-tracked, owner-gated gap P0-02 (paid payment-gateway SDK + credentials, W3) — but being known does not lower severity; the project itself classifies it P0, and the correct interim mitigation (hide /parent/payment behind the surface gate or disclose offline-payment-only) is absent. P0 stands.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** Every cited location checks out. (1) lib/features/parent/payment/parent_payment_provider.dart:104-163 — submitMockPayment() is exactly as described: sets phase to processing, calls initiate then confirm with client-generated 'txn_${DateTime.now().millisecondsSinceEpoch}', then success + PaymentSuccessResult(receiptId/receiptNumber). No gateway checkout UI exists anywhere in lib/features/parent/payment/ (only screen/provider/models/widgets; zero UPI handoff or card-entry code, and the client never sends razorpay_payment_id/signature). (2) lib/router/app_router.dart:371-377 registers /parent/payment; parent_navigation.dart:115-118 pushes it from Pay Now; app_router.dart:2533 wires onPayNow from the fees screen (fee_summary_hero.dart 'Pay Now' button + pay_now_bottom_bar.dart). (3) lib/router/surface_backend_gate.dart:22-40 — the hidden-surface list contains no parent prefix, so the route is reachable in a live API build; the API repo path is real (api_parent_repository.dart:169-184 → parent_remote_datasource.dart POST /parent/payments/confirm → supabase parent_router.ts:52-57). (4) Backend trace goes beyond the citation: payment_service.ts confirmPayment() lines 210-234 explicitly documents that in stub mode (the current default, no RAZORPAY_* env on the pilot) capture proceeds WITHOUT any gateway signature, and lines 236-290 then create a REAL finance collection + receipt against the invoice (createCollection), mark the intent captured, and emit a payment.captured event — i.e., real dues are cleared in the tenant DB with zero money moved. Conversely, if RAZORPAY_STUB_MODE were flipped off, the Flutter client (which sends only transactionRef) would always fail with 'Live payment requires a verified Razorpay payment id and signature' — so the journey is either fake-success (today) or hard-failure (with creds), never a working payment.

### V-06 · CONFIRMED **P1** — Entire web platform is read-only: zero mutation calls, 57/62 action buttons dead
- **Module:** all · **Platform:** web · **Role:** all staff roles · **Reported by:** web-ux-specialist
- **Original evidence:** grep for method POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE across web/src returns only auth calls (AuthContext.tsx:115,127); grep for useMutation/onSubmit finds only the login form (LoginPage.tsx:157). Heuristic count: 57 of 62 primary action buttons (icon add/save/send/payment/edit/etc.) render with no onClick — e.g. 'Collect fee' (finance/CollectionsPage.tsx:46), 'Create exam' (academics/AcademicsPages.tsx:39), 'New broadcast' (engage/EngagePages.tsx:42), 'Add student'/'Import' (sis/StudentRegistryPage.tsx:68-71), 'Apply for leave' (parent/ParentPages.tsx:213), 'Assign substitute' (AcademicsPages.tsx:151). Backend write routes exist and are unused: POST /finance/payments/offline (supabase/functions/_shared/finance/finance_router.ts:320), POST /finance/payments/qr (:341), POST /finance/fee-assignments (:187).
- **Original impact:** No school task can be completed on desktop — the console is a dashboard viewer. Any sales demo that clicks a primary button dead-ends. Contradicts web/PARITY_TRACKER.md's '100% in-scope parity' claim at the capability level.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Build the action layer module-by-module starting with the money and exams paths (collect fee, record offline payment, save marks, approve). Until then, remove or visibly disable dead buttons — a disabled button with a tooltip is honest; an enabled dead button is a trap.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The core claim survives fully: the web platform performs zero non-auth mutations and its primary action buttons are dead while docs claim 100% live-wired parity and a production cert. However, P0 (production-blocking) is inflated. The certified production workflow for the pilot is the Flutter mobile app, which has full write capability — no school task is actually blocked, only the desktop channel. The web UI lane is owner-frozen after a deliberately view-scoped live cert, and several unavailable-state messages show action wiring was consciously deferred. The real defects are (a) capability gap blocking any web commercial launch/sales demo, (b) documentation dishonesty — PARITY_TRACKER's '100% parity/live-wired' claim is false at capability level and the gap is unlogged in VERIFIED_GAPS, and (c) the 'Save marks' button silently discarding user input. These make it a must-fix-before-web-GA P1, not a P0 blocking current production.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** Every cited location verified exactly. web/src contains only two mutation calls, both auth (AuthContext.tsx:115 /auth/login, :127 /auth/verify-otp); the only onSubmit is LoginPage.tsx:157 and there is zero useMutation. All cited buttons render enabled with no onClick: 'Collect fee' (finance/CollectionsPage.tsx:46), 'Create exam' (academics/AcademicsPages.tsx:39), 'New broadcast' (engage/EngagePages.tsx:42), 'Import'/'Add student' (sis/StudentRegistryPage.tsx:68-71), 'Apply for leave' (parent/ParentPages.tsx:213), 'Assign substitute' (AcademicsPages.tsx:~151). Additionally 'Save marks' (AcademicsPages.tsx:90) has onClick={() => setEdits({})} which silently discards entered marks, and 'Contact' (AlumniPages.tsx:81) is dead. Backend write routes confirmed unused: POST /finance/fee-assignments (finance_router.ts:187), POST /finance/payments/qr (:341). PARITY_TRACKER.md claims '100% of in-scope' and 'live-wired' modules; WEB_PRODUCTION_CERTIFICATION.md's PASS covered routes/search/filter/detail only — no write interaction was ever certified. web/VERIFIED_GAPS.md does NOT record the read-only gap (only WEB-001 aggregation, WEB-002 font) and its own rule ('disable only the blocked interaction — honest state') is violated since dead buttons render enabled.

### V-07 · CONFIRMED **P1** — Marks entry 'Save marks' silently discards all entered marks
- **Module:** academics/exams · **Platform:** web · **Role:** teacher / exam admin · **Reported by:** web-ux-specialist
- **Original evidence:** web/src/pages/academics/AcademicsPages.tsx:90 — actions={<Button icon="save" disabled={!examId || !dirty} onClick={() => setEdits({})}>Save marks</Button>}. The handler clears the local edits map; inputs revert to server values (null → placeholder '—'). No POST exists. Compare Flutter: lib/features/academics/exam_admin/exam_marks_entry_screen.dart (960 lines) with verifyCoordinatorResults (:505-509) and submitForApproval (:539-543).
- **Original impact:** A teacher entering marks for 40 students clicks Save and watches every mark vanish. Data loss on the product's declared top-priority module; worse than a missing feature because it simulates one.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Wire save to the marks endpoint with per-row validation (max marks), an unsaved-changes guard on navigation, Enter-to-advance-row, and the frozen AB/ML/DB absent codes (exam-result-status design) which have no web representation at all.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The core claim survives contact with the code: in live mode a teacher can select an exam, type marks into fully enabled inputs, and the enabled Save button destroys all input without any network call — the failure scenario is reproducible as described, and it is worse than the app's other unwired buttons (Create exam, Generate) because those are inert no-ops while this one is dirty-gated and destructive. However, P0 "production-blocking" is inflated for three reasons. (1) The web lane's declared state is a frozen read-only parity recreation — zero business writes exist anywhere in web/src, and page copy throughout says data loads "once the backend is connected"; this is the lane's known scope, not a marks-specific regression. (2) The production marks-entry path for teachers is the Flutter app, which implements the full save/verify/submit/publish flow against the real batch endpoint — the task is fully solvable on the primary platform. (3) In the default demo build the trap is unreachable (button permanently disabled). What keeps it at P1 rather than P2: the web app is live-certified against prod and hosted for role-based review with a live tenant, so the destructive-fake-save is genuinely encounterable there, and an editable grid with an enabled Save that discards work violates basic data-safety UX — it must be fixed (wire the batch POST or disable the inputs/button) before web is treated as a production surface for exams.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** Cited evidence holds verbatim. /Users/surendrakanna/Documents/Akshara_ERP/web/src/pages/academics/AcademicsPages.tsx:90 is exactly `actions={<Button icon="save" disabled={!examId || !dirty} onClick={() => setEdits({})}>Save marks</Button>}`; the edits map (line 71) is the only store for typed marks, inputs render `edits[r.id] ?? (r.marksObtained ?? '')` (line 80), so clearing it reverts every input to server values with no error or confirmation. A repo-wide grep of web/src confirms the ONLY non-GET calls in the entire web app are /auth/login and /auth/verify-otp — no marks POST exists, while the backend endpoint it should call (POST /academics/exams/:id/marks/batch in supabase/functions/_shared/academics/exam_administration/) is real and RBAC-tested. The Flutter comparison also holds: lib/features/academics/exam_admin/exam_marks_entry_screen.dart (960 lines) has real mutations (verifyCoordinatorResults, submitForApproval, processResults via examMarksMutationProvider). One mitigation the reviewer missed: in the default `demo` data mode (web/src/lib/api/config.ts, VITE_DATA_MODE=demo), useModuleQuery returns unavailable, the exam Select never renders, examId stays empty, and the Save button is permanently disabled — the bug is only reachable in `live` mode.

### V-08 · CONFIRMED **P1** — Parent 'Pay now' button does nothing (sets location.hash to empty)
- **Module:** parent portal / fees · **Platform:** web · **Role:** parent · **Reported by:** web-ux-specialist
- **Original evidence:** web/src/pages/parent/ParentPages.tsx:98 — const navPay = () => { window.location.hash = ''; }; wired to the 'Pay now' header action (:102). The /parent/payment page it should reach renders only a disabled 'Proceed to pay' inside a dashed placeholder (ParentPages.tsx:142).
- **Original impact:** The single highest-intent parent action on web is a no-op. Even accepting that the gateway SDK is owner-gated, the button should at minimum navigate to the payment page; today it looks broken.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Point 'Pay now' at /parent/payment via navigate(); on that page show due amount from /parent/fees and an explicit 'online payment coming soon — pay at school office' state instead of a dead dashed box.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The defect is real and cannot be refuted: a visible primary 'Pay now' button in the parent fees page does literally nothing in a BrowserRouter app. No mitigation hides it — the button renders unconditionally in the PageHeader regardless of query state. However, P0 (production-blocking) is inflated for three reasons. (1) The capability behind it is already blocked upstream: online payment requires the paid payment-gateway SDK + credentials (tracked owner-gated P0-02 in the PRA roadmap), so even a correct navigation lands on a deliberately disabled placeholder page — fixing the button restores zero payment capability. (2) Parents are not left without a fee workflow: the Fees page itself fully renders dues/installments, and Receipts show payment history; the actual payment channel for the pilot is offline/school-side (Finance collects), consistent with the module's placeholder copy. (3) The web UI lane is FROZEN after a live certification pass (229/229 routes, crash/blank/401 checks — which this class of dead-handler defect would not trip), so the honest framing is a trust-damaging polish defect to fix at unfreeze, not a launch blocker. Correct fix is one line (navigate('/parent/payment')) or hiding the button until the gateway ships. Confirmed, but P1: real user-facing broken-looking control on a high-intent path, with no functional capability lost relative to what is shippable today.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** All three cited facts verified at the exact locations. web/src/pages/parent/ParentPages.tsx:98 reads `const navPay = () => { window.location.hash = ''; };` and it is wired to the header's primary action at line 102 (`<Button icon="payment" onClick={navPay}>Pay now</Button>`). The app uses createBrowserRouter (web/src/routes/router.tsx:1,533), not hash routing, so setting location.hash to '' is a literal no-op — the button does nothing. A real `/parent/payment` route exists (router.tsx:318 -> ParentPaymentPage), and the correct pattern (`useNavigate`) is used elsewhere in the same file (ParentReceiptsPage:150, ParentMessagesPage:194), so this is a stub/bug, not a routing limitation. ParentPaymentPage (lines 132-147) is itself a dashed-border placeholder whose 'Proceed to pay' button is `disabled` (line 142), stating the gateway loads from /parent/payments/initiate 'once connected'.

### V-09 · CONFIRMED **P1** — Teacher cannot mark attendance: class row click is an empty function, homework form cannot submit
- **Module:** teacher portal · **Platform:** web · **Role:** teacher · **Reported by:** web-ux-specialist
- **Original evidence:** web/src/pages/teacher/TeacherPages.tsx:44 — onRowClick={() => {}} on the 'Mark class attendance' list; no marking screen exists behind it. TeacherHomeworkCreatePage: 'Assign' button has no onClick (:91) and both Selects have options={[]} (:94-95) while the page text claims 'Submitting posts to the live /teacher/homework endpoint' (:100). Backend GET /teacher/attendance/classes exists (supabase/functions/_shared/teacher/teacher_router.ts:83) but no web marking flow consumes it.
- **Original impact:** The two most frequent daily teacher tasks (attendance, homework) dead-end after one click. The false 'posts to the live endpoint' copy actively misleads.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Build a class-roster marking screen (P/A/L toggles, bulk 'all present', save) and wire the homework form; until then remove the claim text and dead-end rows.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The core claim is fully accurate — both teacher write flows on web are non-functional stubs and the endpoint copy is false. But P0 'production-blocking' is inflated: the primary, certified teacher surface is the Flutter app, which implements both flows end-to-end against the same backend, so no teacher is blocked in production. The web app is a secondary parity-recreation lane, currently FROZEN after a render-level route cert and exposed live only as a demo on an isolated test tenant — it is not the production teacher work surface. The gap is a real platform-parity defect plus misleading UI copy that must be fixed before web is offered to teachers as a working tool, which is P1, not a production blocker.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** All cited evidence verified verbatim: web/src/pages/teacher/TeacherPages.tsx:44 has onRowClick={() => {}} with no marking screen anywhere in web/src (route /teacher/attendance -> this stub list only); TeacherHomeworkCreatePage line 91 'Assign' Button has no onClick, lines 94-95 both Selects have options={[]}, line 100 contains the false copy 'Submitting posts to the live /teacher/homework endpoint'; the homework list page's 'Assign homework' button (line 78) is also inert. Backend GET /teacher/attendance/classes exists (teacher_router.ts) and write endpoints POST /teacher/attendance/submit and POST /teacher/homework exist in pilot_operations_router.ts, consumed only by the Flutter app (lib/core/repositories/api/teacher/remote/teacher_api_paths.dart: attendanceSubmit/attendanceDraft), never by web.

### V-10 · CONFIRMED **P1** — Approval Center lists maker-checker approvals but has no approve/reject action
- **Module:** management / governance · **Platform:** web · **Role:** principal / management (checker) · **Reported by:** web-ux-specialist
- **Original evidence:** web/src/pages/management/ManagementPages.tsx:106-124 — ApprovalList renders request, AI recommendation, and status columns via ResourceList with no onRowClick, no actions, no detail route. Same for attendance corrections: web/src/pages/attendance/AttendancePages.tsx:51-67 shows pending correction requests with no action, while its own awaiting-backend copy promises 'Approve/reject posts to the live maker-checker endpoint' (:66).
- **Original impact:** Every governance flow the product is proudest of (fee concessions FIN-D4, stock write-downs, dues waivers, attendance corrections) has no checker UI on the platform where checkers actually work — desktops. Approvals pile up unless the approver uses a phone.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Add row-level Approve/Reject with a confirm dialog and reason capture; this is the single highest-leverage write feature for desktop.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The core claim survives contact with the code: on the web platform there is no functional checker action for ANY maker-checker flow — Approval Center, attendance corrections, refunds, leave, admissions — and the dashboard even shows non-functional approve/reject buttons, which is arguably a worse UX sin. Attempts to refute via mitigations failed: there is no alternate web route, detail page, or mutation path anywhere in web/src. However, P0 (production-blocking) is inflated for three reasons. (1) The production checker surface today is the Flutter app, which has a complete, guarded approve/reject flow hitting real decision endpoints — no governance flow is actually blocked in production; the finding's own impact statement concedes this ('unless the approver uses a phone'). (2) The web app is not serving production checkers: per project state, the web UI lane was owner-frozen after a route-rendering live cert, and the web app's only live deployment is a UX-review demo on an isolated test tenant — production pilot users are on Flutter. (3) The read-only state is a self-documented, consistent scaffold pattern ('once the backend is connected…') across every approval page, i.e. a known parity-phase gap, not a silent regression in a shipped checker flow. It is a real, must-fix-before-web-GA gap — a desktop 'Approval Center' that can only watch approvals pile up would be indefensible in production — so it is confirmed at P1: blocks the web lane from ever being declared a production checker surface, but does not block current production.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** The cited evidence is accurate and actually understated. web/src/pages/management/ManagementPages.tsx:106-124 — ApprovalList renders a ResourceList with columns only (request/type/requester/amount/AI/status), no onRowClick, no actions prop, no mutation; the same component backs both ApprovalCenterPage and ManagementTasksPage. Worse, the ManagementDashboardPage pending-approvals card (lines 59-60) renders check/close icon buttons with NO onClick handler at all — decorative dead buttons. web/src/pages/attendance/AttendancePages.tsx:51-67 matches the claim exactly, including the awaiting-backend copy at :66 promising 'Approve/reject posts to the live maker-checker endpoint.' A repo-wide grep of web/src found zero approve/reject POST or mutation calls anywhere — finance/RefundsPage.tsx:37, admissions/ApprovalPage.tsx:46, and hr/LeavePage.tsx:37 all carry the same 'posts to the live decision endpoint' promise with no wiring. By contrast, the Flutter app has a fully functional checker flow: lib/core/repositories/api/approval/remote/approval_api_paths.dart defines POST …/approve and …/reject (plus PRI-1 batch decisions), and lib/features/management/approval/widgets/approval_queue_table.dart wires real Approve/Reject actions with confirm dialogs and a maker-checker self-approval guard (Approve disabled for the maker).

### V-11 · CONFIRMED **P1** — Flagship Exams module missing from Admin Hub cards and mobile bottom nav (workspace set omission)
- **Module:** admin / exams / navigation · **Platform:** all-mobile · **Role:** principal, schoolAdmin, vicePrincipal, management · **Reported by:** enterprise-saas-designer
- **Original evidence:** lib/core/workspace/workspace.dart:67-85 — schoolAdministration workspace module set lists 16 modules but NOT AdminModule.exams. lib/features/admin/admin_navigation_provider.dart:58-68 added Exams as a top-level destination ('A5 — un-bury the flagship Exam Administration module'), but the workspace filter (lines 272-284) drops it again for every surface that uses workspaceScopedNavDestinationsProvider: the Admin Hub module cards (admin_hub_screen.dart:28) and the phone bottom nav (admin_bottom_nav.dart:47). activeWorkspaceProvider never resolves null for staff (workspace_providers.dart:24-34), so the filter always applies. Visually confirmed: test/golden/goldens/dark_admin_hub_834x1194.png shows exactly 14 module cards — Marketing, Alumni, Director all present, Exams absent.
- **Original impact:** The owner-declared top-priority module is invisible on the two primary mobile surfaces (hub landing + bottom nav) and reachable only via the hamburger drawer, which uses the UNSCOPED provider (admin_navigation_rail.dart:56) — so the drawer and the hub show different module sets in the same session, undermining trust in the nav. Exam marks entry on mobile costs 4+ taps (More drawer → Exams → exam → marks), failing the 3-click rule for the flagship workflow.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Add AdminModule.exams to the schoolAdministration workspace module set (one line), then reconcile the drawer and hub to use the same workspace-scoped provider so the two surfaces can never diverge.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The core defect is real and confirmed: the deliberate A5 fix to surface the flagship Exams module is silently defeated on the Admin Hub card grid for all school-administration roles, and the hub and drawer show inconsistent module sets in the same session. However, it is not production-blocking: no exam workflow is broken or unreachable — Exams remains 2 taps away via the More/drawer path (which correctly lists it) plus a pre-existing in-page shortcut, and the bottom-nav claim is largely moot since Exams would not fit the 4 visible tabs even after the fix. This is a high-priority discoverability/consistency defect with a one-line fix (add AdminModule.exams to the schoolAdministration set), warranting P1, not P0.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** All code citations verified exactly as claimed: workspace.dart:67-85 schoolAdministration module set (16 modules) omits AdminModule.exams; admin_navigation_provider.dart:61-68 adds Exams top-level with the A5 'un-bury the flagship' comment; lines 272-284 workspace filter drops any module not in the set (only AdminModule.admin exempt); admin_hub_screen.dart:28 and admin_bottom_nav.dart:47 both consume the scoped provider; admin_navigation_rail.dart:56 (the drawer, via admin_shell.dart:56-58) consumes the UNSCOPED provider, so drawer shows Exams while hub does not; kRoleWorkspaces maps principal/schoolAdmin/vicePrincipal/management to schoolAdministration and activeWorkspaceProvider never returns null for them. Golden dark_admin_hub_834x1194.png shows exactly 14 cards (labels are obfuscated block-text so specific module names cannot be read, but the count is consistent with Exams absent). Two overstatements found: (1) AdminBottomNav shows only the first 4 destinations and Exams ranks 6th, so Exams would not be a visible bottom-nav tab even if the workspace set included it — the bottom-nav omission is effectively moot; (2) Exams is reachable in 2 taps (More tab → drawer → Exams), not 'only via hamburger' at 4+ taps to the module, because the More button opens the unscoped drawer.

### V-12 · CONFIRMED **P1** — Entire academic-operations cluster orphaned on mobile — 'School Completion' hub has zero inbound links
- **Module:** school_completion / academics setup · **Platform:** all-mobile · **Role:** schoolAdmin, principal · **Reported by:** enterprise-saas-designer
- **Original evidence:** Route-graph analysis: RouteNames.schoolCompletionHub ('/school/completion') is registered (app_router.dart:785-788) but no screen or nav surface links to it (grep across lib/ returns only the router registration). Its hub screen (lib/features/school_completion/school_completion_hub_screen.dart:14-141) is the ONLY inbound linker for ~20 destinations including Class Teachers ('Set one class teacher per section (takes attendance)', line 79-83), Subject Assignments, Timetable Automation, Lesson Logs, Room Allocation, Syllabus Automation, Broadcast Admin, Communication Delivery/Analytics, Parent Activation. substituteManager's only other inbound is timetable_optimization_screen.dart:174 — itself reachable only from the orphan hub. The web app DOES expose the cluster via Configure → 'Setup & Ops' (web/src/routes/navConfig.ts:78 → /school-completion).
- **Original impact:** Foundational setup workflows — assigning the class teacher who takes attendance, generating timetables, subject assignment, communication broadcasts — cannot be reached through any navigation in the mobile-first Flutter app. A school admin onboarding from a phone or tablet cannot complete school setup; the same tasks are reachable on web only, silently contradicting the mobile-first promise.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Give the cluster a real home: either add an 'Academics Setup' card/destination to the schoolAdministration workspace, or fold its items into the Exams/SIS/Management IA. Also rename the user-facing title 'School Completion' (internal program name) to task language like 'School Setup'.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The core claim survives adversarial contact: on the Flutter (mobile) app the School Completion hub is a registered but nav-orphaned route, and ~17 of its 21 destinations — including Class Teachers (which gates attendance), Subject Management/Assignments, Timetable Automation, Lesson Logs, Syllabus Automation, Room Allocation — have no other inbound navigation. Flutter mobile has no URL bar, so route registration alone means unreachable for real users. This is a genuine, severe navigation defect, and the in-code A5 comment shows the pattern (buried modules) was already recognized for Exam Administration but not fixed for the rest. However, P0 'production-blocking' is inflated: the identical cluster is fully exposed to the same schoolAdmin/principal roles in the live-certified web app (navConfig.ts:78, 229/229-route live cert), so the business tasks ARE achievable in production; these are one-time/occasional admin setup workflows, not daily-use flows for the broad user base; and four of the listed destinations (Exam Administration, Broadcast Admin, Communication Analytics, Teacher Reassignment) do have mobile inbound paths the reviewer missed. Real, must-fix-before-GA mobile-parity gap given the mobile-first North Star, but a functioning certified alternative path for the affected roles means it is P1, not P0.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** Every load-bearing citation checks out. (1) /Users/surendrakanna/Documents/Akshara_ERP/lib/router/app_router.dart:785-788 registers RouteNames.schoolCompletionHub exactly as claimed; the only other references in lib/ are route_names.dart:120/599 and route_guards.dart:70 (a permission guard, not a nav surface). Zero `context.push/go` calls target '/school/completion' anywhere in lib/. (2) The hub screen (lib/features/school_completion/school_completion_hub_screen.dart:14-144) links exactly 21 destinations, including Class Teachers at lines 77-83 with the quoted subtitle. (3) Per-route inbound audit: classTeacherAssignments, subjectsManagement, subjectAssignments, timetableAutomation, timetableOptimization, substituteManager, lessonLogs, lessonAnalytics, roomAllocation, syllabusAutomation, communicationDelivery, parentActivationDashboard, pilotDashboard, whatsAppProvider, schoolBranding, academicProgress, timetableIntelligence have NO inbound link outside the orphaned hub. Neither admin_navigation_provider.dart (bottom nav/rail), workspace.dart homeRoutes, nor global_search_registry.dart include them. (4) Web mitigation confirmed: web/src/routes/navConfig.ts:78 exposes 'Setup & Ops' → /school-completion under Configure. Minor evidence errors: the claimed substituteManager inbound at timetable_optimization_screen.dart:174 does not exist at that path (grep shows substituteManager has zero non-hub inbound), and the reviewer missed four partial mitigations — examAdministration was deliberately promoted to a top-level admin nav entry (admin_navigation_provider.dart:59-63, comment 'A5 — un-bury... it was only reachable via an in-page shortcut', proving the team knew this hub buries features), communicationBroadcastAdmin is reachable from parent_meeting_detail_screen.dart:98, communicationAnalytics via global_search_registry.dart:174, and teacherReassignment via intelligence_recommendation_navigation.dart:31.

### V-13 · CONFIRMED **P1** — Web: Copilot is a first-class sidebar item for every staff role but resolves to a brochure page with a disabled button
- **Module:** copilot / navigation · **Platform:** web · **Role:** all staff roles · **Reported by:** enterprise-saas-designer
- **Original evidence:** web/src/routes/navConfig.ts:33 puts 'Copilot' in the Overview section gated on moduleKey 'dashboard', which every staff role holds (roles.ts ROLE_META — even storekeeper has 'dashboard'). web/src/routes/router.tsx:470 maps '/copilot' to Misc.CopilotPage; web/src/pages/misc/MiscPages.tsx:37 defines CopilotPage as InfoPage('Akshara Copilot', … action='New chat') where InfoPage renders the action as <Button … disabled> (MiscPages.tsx:16). The same InfoPage brochure pattern backs '/ai-assistant', SetupWizardPage, AiAssistantSettingsPage and the dynamic-widget pages. The Flutter app, by contrast, routes /copilot to a real copilot experience.
- **Original impact:** One of the five 'Overview' items every staff user sees is a static marketing card whose only button is permanently disabled — a guaranteed dead-end in the first minute of any web demo, and a parity break with the mobile app the web was built to mirror.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Remove Copilot from the web sidebar until the real experience ships (hide-first is the house rule), or wire it to the actual AI gateway. Audit all InfoPage-backed routes reachable from visible nav and apply the same rule.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The finding is factually correct and cannot be refuted on evidence — a first-class Overview sidebar item shown to every staff role resolves to a static brochure card whose only button is disabled, while the mobile app it mirrors has a real copilot. However, P0 (production-blocking) is inflated. Nothing is broken or unsafe: no crash, no blank page, no data risk, no workflow that users can start and fail — the page is an explicitly 'honest state' placeholder (MiscPages.tsx:11 comment) that truthfully describes an unbuilt feature. The web lane passed a live certification (229/229 routes, 0 crash/blank/401) with this pattern in place and was frozen by the owner with only the token-refresh P1 left open, i.e., this design was seen and accepted at cert time. The legitimate residual issue is product quality and parity: a dead-end item in the top nav section for all staff roles is a poor first-run/demo experience and a genuine mobile-parity gap, which merits P1 (fix before commercial demos/GA — either wire it to the AI gateway like Flutter or drop/badge the nav item), not a production blocker.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** Every cited location checks out. web/src/routes/navConfig.ts:33 has { key: 'copilot', label: 'Copilot', path: '/copilot', moduleKey: 'dashboard' } in the 'Overview' section of ERP_NAV. roles.ts ROLE_META confirms every staff role holds 'dashboard' (admin/principal/management have modules '*'; financeAdmin, admissionsCounselor, transportManager, hostelManager, librarian, inventoryManager, and storekeeper at roles.ts:75-78 all list 'dashboard' explicitly). router.tsx:470 maps '/copilot' to Misc.CopilotPage. MiscPages.tsx:37 defines CopilotPage via InfoPage with action='New chat', and MiscPages.tsx:15 renders that action as <Button icon="bolt" disabled> — permanently disabled, no handler. The same InfoPage pattern backs '/ai-assistant' (CopilotPersonaShellPage, router.tsx:471), SetupWizardPage, AiAssistantSettingsPage, and the dynamic-widget pages, as claimed. The Flutter parity claim also holds: lib/router/app_router.dart:603-606 routes RouteNames.copilot through copilotRouteBuilder to lib/features/copilot/copilot_screen.dart, a 379-line real chat screen with a TextField, _sendMessage(), and a copilotSendMessageProvider hitting the AI gateway. No hidden mitigation found on web: no other web route provides a working general copilot (only module-scoped FinanceCopilotPage/InventoryCopilotPage exist), and nothing hides the nav item from users.

### V-14 · CONFIRMED **P1** — Admissions→Finance fee handoff silently discards the clerk's fee-structure selection
- **Module:** admissions/finance handoff · **Platform:** all-mobile · **Role:** Admissions clerk · **Reported by:** hci-specialist
- **Original evidence:** lib/features/admissions/fee_handoff/widgets/admissions_fee_handoff_panel.dart:81-96 — the 'Fee structure' DropdownMenu's onSelected is null unless onFeeStructureChanged is passed; lib/features/admissions/fee_handoff/admissions_fee_handoff_screen.dart:63-74 and 106-117 — BOTH call-sites omit onFeeStructureChanged, and onSendToFinance falls back to `selected.selectedFeeStructureId ?? feeStructures.first.id`. Flutter's DropdownMenu updates its own visible text on selection even with a null onSelected, so the UI confirms a choice that is never stored.
- **Original impact:** The clerk picks fee structure B, sees B in the field, taps 'Send to Finance', and the system silently sends structure A (first in list). A student can be enrolled on the wrong fee plan with no error, on the product's flagship cross-module journey. Silent wrong-money assignment.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Wire onFeeStructureChanged into a provider that updates the handoff's selectedFeeStructureId, and make 'Send to Finance' hard-require an explicit selection (disabled until chosen) instead of falling back to feeStructures.first.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The defect is real: the admissions fee-structure dropdown visibly accepts a selection that is never stored, and Send to Finance transmits the stale stored value with no warning — a genuine misleading-UI bug on the flagship cross-module journey. But P0 requires the claimed impact ('student enrolled on wrong fee plan silently') to hold, and it does not: the handoff value is only a recommendation that pre-fills an editable, permission-gated Finance fee-assignment screen where a Finance user must actively pick/confirm the structure and press Generate before any fee account or invoice exists. The risk is default-anchoring (Finance rubber-stamps the wrong pre-selected plan), which is serious but mediated by a second human checkpoint in a different role. The 'sends first in list' framing is also overstated — it sends the previously stored selection, falling back to first only when null. Real bug, wrong-money risk is indirect: P1, not production-blocking P0.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** Every cited line checks out. admissions_fee_handoff_panel.dart:81-96 wires DropdownMenu.onSelected to null whenever onFeeStructureChanged is null; Flutter's DropdownMenu stays enabled and updates its visible text on selection even with null onSelected, so the UI confirms an unstored choice. admissions_fee_handoff_screen.dart:63-74 and 106-117 both omit onFeeStructureChanged (repo-wide grep: NO caller passes it anywhere) and both send `selected.selectedFeeStructureId ?? feeStructures.first.id`. runSendToFinance (admissions_workflow_actions.dart:346-367) has no confirmation dialog and the API repository transmits the value for real. However, the reviewer missed the downstream mitigation: finance_fee_assignment_screen.dart:244-405 uses the handed-off structure only as a pre-selected DEFAULT in an editable Fee structure dropdown (working onSelected), behind Permission.manageFinance, requiring an explicit 'Generate student fee account' action by the Finance role before any account exists, plus a Cancel-assignment path; the mock maps the field to `recommendedFeePlanId`. Also, the fallback usually sends the stored per-student selectedFeeStructureId, not literally 'first in list' (first only when null).

### V-15 · CONFIRMED **P1** — Web ERP is a read-only shell: every primary workflow action is a dead button
- **Module:** web (all modules) · **Platform:** web · **Role:** Cashier / Teacher / Admin on desktop · **Reported by:** hci-specialist
- **Original evidence:** Only POSTs in the entire web app are auth (web/src/lib/auth/AuthContext.tsx:115,127 — /auth/login, /auth/verify-otp); zero useMutation/POST in any page. 42 `<Button icon="add">` primary actions with no onClick (e.g. web/src/pages/finance/CollectionsPage.tsx:46 'Collect fee', web/src/pages/library/CatalogPage.tsx:39 'Add book', web/src/pages/teacher/TeacherPages.tsx:78 'Assign homework', :182 'Apply for leave'). TeacherPages.tsx:43-44 attendance rows wired to `onRowClick={() => {}}`. TeacherPages.tsx:87-104 'Assign homework' form has empty Select options, a dead 'Assign' button, and copy claiming 'Submitting posts to the live /teacher/homework endpoint'.
- **Original impact:** No fee can be collected, no attendance marked, no marks entered, no leave applied, nothing approved on web. Buttons render enabled and styled as primary CTAs, so users click and get silence — the worst feedback failure class. The 'once the backend is connected' copy contradicts the enabled buttons. This would fail instantly in a sales demo at a desk.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Either wire the mutations (the Supabase endpoints exist — the Flutter app calls them) or remove/disable the dead CTAs and label the web app read-only. Never render an enabled primary button with no handler.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The finding is real and precisely evidenced — nothing to refute on the facts, and it IS the worst feedback class (enabled filled primary CTAs that do nothing, plus one line of copy falsely claiming live submission). Severity is the only overreach. P0 means production-blocking, and the mitigations the reviewer omits are: (a) all transacting workflows (fee collection, attendance, marks, approvals) run on the certified Flutter mobile app, which is the actual production surface for the live pilot — no real user is currently unable to collect a fee; (b) the web lane was owner-frozen after a render/route cert and is deployed only as a UX-review demo on an isolated test tenant, i.e., web was consciously shipped read-first ('once the backend is connected' copy appears on nearly every page, disclosing the state); (c) this is a known lane boundary, not a hidden regression. It absolutely blocks any web GA, desktop-cashier use, or sales demo of web as a working client, and the dead-but-enabled buttons (they should at minimum be disabled or navigate to a stub) plus the false /teacher/homework copy are genuine defects that must be fixed before the web lane unfreezes. That is a top-priority P1 tracked against web write-path enablement, not a P0 against current production.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** Every cited location checks out exactly. (1) web/src/lib/auth/AuthContext.tsx:115,127 are the only POSTs in web/src — a repo-wide grep for useMutation / method:'POST' / .post( finds nothing else. (2) There are exactly 42 `<Button icon="add">` instances in web/src/pages; only 3 have onClick, and all 3 merely navigate() to list pages whose own add-buttons are dead (admin/DashboardPage.tsx:58, AdmissionsDashboardPage.tsx:33, FinanceDashboardPage.tsx:46 -> /finance/collections, whose 'Collect fee' at CollectionsPage.tsx:46 has no handler). (3) CatalogPage.tsx:39 'Add book' — no onClick. (4) TeacherPages.tsx:43-44 attendance ResourceList wired to `onRowClick={() => {}}`; :78 'Assign homework' dead; :91 'Assign' dead; :94-95 Selects with `options={[]}`; :100 copy literally says 'Submitting posts to the live /teacher/homework endpoint' while nothing submits; :182 'Apply for leave' dead. (5) components/ui/Button.tsx is a plain <button> with no default handler — no hidden wiring. (6) The one non-navigation workflow onClick found, AcademicsPages.tsx:90 'Save marks', only runs setEdits({}) — no persistence. The core claim (web is read-only; enabled primary CTAs are silent no-ops; copy contradicts UI) is fully accurate and not inflated on the facts.

### V-16 · CONFIRMED **P0** — Parent 'Pay now' completes a simulated payment with a real-looking receipt
- **Module:** parent/payments · **Platform:** all-mobile · **Role:** Parent · **Reported by:** hci-specialist
- **Original evidence:** lib/features/parent/payment/parent_payment_screen.dart:115 — `onPay: () => submitMockPayment(ref)`; lib/features/parent/payment/parent_payment_provider.dart:104-112 — mock flow with a parentPaymentSimulateFailureProvider switch; success view then shows 'Payment successful' + receipt number (parent_payment_screen.dart:338-347).
- **Original impact:** The parent's primary money journey ends in a fake gateway that renders a success ceremony and receipt without moving money. Known owner-gated dependency (payment-gateway SDK), but as shipped the journey is a production-blocking simulation.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Gate the 'Pay now' CTA off entirely (with a 'pay at school office' explainer) until the real gateway lands; never show the success ceremony from a mock path in production builds.
- **Verifier reasoning:** Attempted refutation fails on every axis. (1) Evidence is accurate at all cited lines. (2) Mitigations: the backend does have a fail-closed guard — with stubMode=false, confirm without a verified Razorpay payment id + signature throws before any capture/receipt. But that guard only bites if an operator flips the env; the SHIPPED default is stub mode, and the mobile client cannot produce a real gateway payment anyway (no SDK, fabricated txn ref), so the guard does not rescue the user journey — it would just turn every payment into a hard failure. (3) Severity is honest, arguably understated: in the as-shipped default configuration, a parent tapping 'Pay now' causes the backend to post a genuine finance collection and issue a genuine numbered receipt (APS-YYYY-...) against the invoice with zero money moved — worse than a cosmetic fake receipt, it corrupts real financial records and clears dues for free. That is production-blocking for the parent money journey by any definition, which matches the project's own tracked P0-02 (payment-gateway SDK, owner-gated external dependency). (4) Not a scope error: the fake success ceremony is user-facing on all mobile. The only caveat is framing: the reviewer's word 'mock' undersells it (real DB writes, simulated gateway), and the gap is already known/tracked as owner-gated P0-02 — so it is a confirmed, previously-acknowledged P0, not a new discovery.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** Every cited location holds. lib/features/parent/payment/parent_payment_screen.dart:115 is exactly `onPay: () => submitMockPayment(ref)`. parent_payment_provider.dart:104-112 is `submitMockPayment` with the `parentPaymentSimulateFailureProvider` test switch (declared at lines 26-28). The success view (screen lines 338-347) renders `AksharaSuccessView` with 'Payment successful', the amount, and 'Receipt <receiptNumber>'. One correction to the reviewer's characterization: this is NOT a purely client-side mock. submitMockPayment calls real repository mutations (initiateParentPaymentProvider / confirmParentPaymentProvider, lib/features/parent/parent_mutations_provider.dart:314/346) that hit the backend Universal Payment Engine (supabase/functions/_shared/payment/payment_service.ts). That backend runs in Razorpay STUB MODE by default (razorpay_config.ts:15 — `RAZORPAY_STUB_MODE` defaults to "true", and it is also forced on whenever RAZORPAY_KEY_ID/SECRET are absent). In stub mode, confirmPayment (payment_service.ts:219 comment block) explicitly skips gateway signature verification, then creates a REAL finance collection + REAL receipt (createCollection, lines 240-252) and marks the intent captured — with the client sending only a fabricated `transactionRef: 'txn_<millis>'` (provider line 139). The Flutter app contains no Razorpay checkout/SDK integration at all; there is no alternate parent payment route.

### V-17 · CONFIRMED **P1** — Web DataTable rows and sort headers are mouse-only — keyboard users cannot open records on core list pages
- **Module:** shared web UI (DataTable) — affects SIS, Admissions, HR, Finance, Teacher, Parent, Alumni · **Platform:** web · **Role:** any keyboard-only or switch-access user (admin/clerk personas most affected) · **Reported by:** accessibility-specialist
- **Original evidence:** web/src/components/ui/DataTable.tsx:103-109 — <tr onClick={() => onRowClick?.(row)}> with no tabIndex, no role, no onKeyDown; :81-91 — sortable <th onClick={toggleSort}> is not a button, no keyboard path, no aria-sort. onRowClick drives navigation on 8 core pages: web/src/pages/sis/StudentRegistryPage.tsx, admissions/LeadsPage.tsx, admissions/ApprovalPage.tsx, hr/EmployeesPage.tsx, finance/CollectionsPage.tsx, teacher/TeacherPages.tsx, parent/ParentPages.tsx, alumni/AlumniPages.tsx
- **Original impact:** The list-to-detail pattern is the web ERP's primary journey. A keyboard-only user can reach a table but can never open a student, lead, approval, employee, or collection record, and can never sort. This is a hard WCAG 2.1.1 failure that blocks daily workflows and would fail any enterprise accessibility procurement review (VPAT).
- **Reviewer recommendation:** In DataTable: render sortable headers as <button> inside <th> with aria-sort; give clickable rows tabIndex={0}, role="button" (or a real link in the first cell), Enter/Space key handling, and a focus-visible style. One component fix repairs all 8+ pages.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The core defect is real and I could not refute it: row activation and column sort in the shared DataTable are mouse-only, there is no per-row focusable element anywhere in web/src, and drawer-based detail views are pure local state so no URL workaround exists. It is a genuine WCAG 2.1.1 Level A failure on the list-to-detail pattern. But P0 (production-blocking) is inflated for this codebase's context: (1) the impact is confined to keyboard-only/switch-access users — mouse and touch users, i.e. the entire current pilot population of this mobile-first product, complete every workflow normally, so no daily workflow is blocked in production today; (2) two of the eight cited pages (ApprovalPage, Teacher Attendance) are no-op onRowClick placeholders where nothing is lost, and several other cited tables are still backend-unconnected placeholder surfaces, so the truly affected live surface is smaller than claimed; (3) the fix is trivial and fully centralized in one shared component (add tabIndex/role/onKeyDown to tr, wrap th content in a button with aria-sort), not an architectural gap. This is a must-fix-before-GA/procurement accessibility defect on the primary web journey — squarely P1 (also note the web UI lane is owner-frozen, so the fix needs an unfreeze) — but it does not meet the bar of production-blocking P0.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** Evidence verified at the cited locations. /Users/surendrakanna/Documents/Akshara_ERP/web/src/components/ui/DataTable.tsx lines 103-110: <tr key=... onClick={() => onRowClick?.(row)}> with cursor-pointer/hover styling and NO tabIndex, NO role, NO onKeyDown. Lines 81-96: sortable <th onClick={() => toggleSort(c)}> is a plain th (not a button, no tabindex, no onKeyDown, no aria-sort); sort is genuinely unreachable by keyboard. All 8 cited pages do pass onRowClick, and I confirmed there is NO alternate keyboard path: a repo-wide grep for the per-row actions function form (actions={(row)=>...}) on DataTable returns zero hits — the actions= usages on those pages are page-header buttons (Add alumnus, Collect fee), not row-level focusable links. Drawer-based pages (StudentRegistryPage:164, EmployeesPage:116, LeadsPage:126, AlumniPages:75) open the detail via local setSelected state with no route, so even direct-URL entry cannot substitute. Two scope errors in the claim, though: ApprovalPage.tsx:44 and TeacherPages.tsx:44 pass onRowClick={() => {}} — a no-op that does nothing for mouse users either, so those two pages are not examples of blocked navigation; and several of the affected ResourceLists (CollectionsPage, teacher/parent/alumni lists) carry 'once the backend is connected' unavailable states, i.e. parts of the cited surface currently render placeholder content rather than live records.

### V-18 · CONFIRMED **P0** — Fee counter can post a payment against the wrong student's invoice
- **Module:** finance · **Platform:** all · **Role:** Fee clerk / cashier · **Reported by:** school-erp-domain-expert
- **Original evidence:** lib/features/finance/finance_workflow_actions.dart:728-762 — the Record-collection dialog builds its invoice picker from financeInvoicesProvider (ALL school invoices, page 1 pageSize 200 per lib/features/finance/invoices/finance_invoices_provider.dart:16) and preselects journeyInvoice ?? defaultInvoiceId('inv_1') ?? invoices.first. Labels (lines 734-738) are 'INV-nnn · Term · ₹X due' with NO student name. When opened from a student's account via 'Collect fee' (lib/features/finance/student_accounts/finance_student_accounts_screen.dart:267-275) only defaultAmount/studentLabel are passed — the invoice list is never scoped to that student.
- **Original impact:** The highest-frequency daily workflow (fee counter: search student -> collect -> receipt) prefills student A's balance while preselecting an arbitrary, possibly different student's invoice; the cashier cannot tell because invoice labels carry no student name, and a busy counter will hit Record payment. Money posts to the wrong account. Beyond mis-posting, a student whose invoice is not in the first 200 school-wide invoices cannot be collected against at all.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** When the collection originates from a student account, filter the picker to that student's open invoices and preselect the oldest due; always render the student name + admission number in the invoice option label; remove the 'inv_1' fallback.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The core claim survives contact with the code completely, and inspection revealed aggravating factors the reviewer understated: the student-context header plus prefilled correct amount actively reassure the cashier while the payment target is an arbitrary school-wide invoice, and the amount posted is student A's balance regardless of which invoice is selected. No mitigating route, guard, or scoped picker exists anywhere in the student-accounts flow. This is a default-path money mis-posting in the highest-frequency cashier workflow — squarely a money-integrity P0 by this project's own standards (PRA treats money-integrity defects as P0). The only inaccuracy is 'platform: all' (web has no record-collection flow), which does not change severity since the Flutter app is the primary counter tool.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** Every cited line holds. finance_workflow_actions.dart:728-762: dialog reads financeInvoicesProvider (unscoped, all-school) and preselects journeyInvoice ?? 'inv_1' ?? invoices.first; journeyInvoice is set by the fee-assignment mutation (finance_mutations_provider.dart:348) and can belong to a different student. Labels (736-737) are 'invoiceNumber · termLabel · outstanding due' with no student name, though FinanceInvoice carries studentId (finance_models.dart:447) so scoping was possible. finance_invoices_provider.dart:15-17 confirms page 1 / pageSize 200 hard cap. finance_student_accounts_screen.dart:267-275 confirms 'Collect fee' passes only defaultAmount/studentLabel/duesLabel — no studentId or invoice scoping — and the screen contains no invoice list (grep: zero 'invoice' hits), so no alternative per-invoice collect route exists; the only other caller is the generic collections screen. Worse than claimed: defaultAmount (student A's balance) overrides the preselected invoice's outstanding (lines 768-770), and the dialog renders student A's name/admission as a header (line 1040), so an untouched 'Record payment' posts A's balance to an arbitrary invoice via CreateCollectionRequest(invoiceId) (816-822) with no client-side invoice-belongs-to-student guard. One scope correction: platform is NOT 'all' — web/src/pages/finance/CollectionsPage.tsx is a read-only list with no record dialog; the defect is Flutter-only.

### V-19 · CONFIRMED **P1** — Web ERP has no fee-collection write path at all
- **Module:** finance · **Platform:** web · **Role:** Fee clerk (desktop counter) · **Reported by:** school-erp-domain-expert
- **Original evidence:** web/src/pages/finance/CollectionsPage.tsx:46 — actions={<Button icon="add">Collect fee</Button>} has NO onClick handler; web/src/pages/finance/FinanceDashboardPage.tsx:46-48 — the dashboard 'Collect fee' button merely navigates to /finance/collections (the page with the dead button). web/src/pages/finance/StudentAccountsPage.tsx has no collect action (grep found zero collect handlers across web/src).
- **Original impact:** The desktop web app — the natural device for a school fee counter — is read-only for the single most important daily finance workflow. The button renders, invites a click, and does nothing: a broken primary journey that would fail the first minute of a sales demo. The claimed 1:1 Flutter parity does not hold on the money path.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Either implement the collect dialog on web (scoped to a searched student) or remove the dead 'Collect fee' buttons and label the surface read-only until the write path ships.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The core claim survives fully — the web app is read-only on the money path and the primary 'Collect fee' CTA is a dead active button, which violates the web track's own rule to disable blocked interactions with an honest state, and the excuse of 'wire once the backend lands' no longer applies because the backend write endpoints exist. However P0 (production-blocking) is inflated: (1) the web lane is an owner-frozen, screen-parity phase whose production certification explicitly covered GET wiring/navigation/RBAC, never write flows; (2) the live pilot's certified fee-collection path is the Flutter app, which has a complete write stack (finance_mutations_provider.dart, finance_payments_provider.dart, QR/offline payment screens), so no real fee clerk is currently blocked; (3) the web app is presently deployed only as an isolated UX-review demo tenant, not as a school's fee counter of record. Real, unregistered defect on a primary journey plus an overstated parity claim in the cert language → confirmed at P1, escalating to P0 only if/when the web app is positioned as the production desktop counter.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** All cited evidence verified exactly as claimed. CollectionsPage.tsx:46 has actions={<Button icon="add">Collect fee</Button>} with no onClick AND no disabled prop — an active-looking dead button. FinanceDashboardPage.tsx:46-48 confirmed: 'Collect fee' merely navigates to /finance/collections. Grep of all web/src shows the ONLY POST calls in the entire web app are /auth/login and /auth/verify-otp (AuthContext.tsx:115,127); zero finance mutations exist anywhere, plus a second dead 'Record payment' button in FinanceExtraPages.tsx:45. StudentAccountsPage has no collect action. Backend write endpoints DO exist (docs/roadmap/WEB_TRACK_BACKEND_GAPS.md:67 — finance write/maker-checker endpoints routed in _shared/finance/finance_router.ts), so this is a client wiring gap, and it is NOT listed in the web track's open-gaps register (VERIFIED_GAPS.md lists only WEB-001/WEB-002).

### V-20 · CONFIRMED **P1** — Admissions 'View' button silently submits a draft application; no application detail screen exists
- **Module:** admissions · **Platform:** all-mobile · **Role:** Admissions clerk / counselor · **Reported by:** school-erp-domain-expert
- **Original evidence:** lib/features/admissions/applications/admissions_applications_screen.dart:125-148 — _handleApplicationAction: if status==draft it immediately calls submitApplicationProvider.execute (no confirmation); for any other status it shows only a snackbar with the status. The triggering control is labeled 'View' / tooltip 'View application' (lib/features/admissions/applications/widgets/admissions_applications_table.dart:70-71, 131-132). No route or screen renders application contents anywhere in lib/features/admissions/.
- **Original impact:** Tapping the only affordance on an application row performs an irreversible workflow mutation the clerk believed was read-only — a half-entered draft gets submitted for approval. And since no detail view exists, staff can never re-open an application to check the child's details, class sought, or documents before approving: the approval queue asks them to approve what they cannot inspect.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Build an application detail screen (profile, documents, status history, edit-while-draft); make 'View' navigate to it; make Submit an explicit, confirmed action on that screen.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The core defect is real and reproduced in code: the sole affordance on an application row is labeled 'View' but silently performs an unconfirmed, unrecoverable workflow mutation (draft→submitted) with no completeness check, and there is genuinely no application detail screen — a clerk literally cannot look at a draft without submitting it. That survives contact with the code completely, so confirmed=true. Severity is honest-but-inflated at P0: the mutation loses no data (all summary fields remain visible in the row/card), triggers no financial or security consequence, and lands the application in a human approval queue where AD-07 provides a summary review panel and an explicit Reject action — so the 'approve what they cannot inspect' half of the impact is materially mitigated, and recovery (reject + recreate from the lead) exists, if clumsy.
