- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** parent_transport_screen.dart:74-81 exists exactly as cited: const AksharaInsightCard with hardcoded 'Bus is approximately 8 minutes away (telemetry preview). Live map integration is not enabled in this build.' and actionLabel 'Refresh ETA' with no onAction; rendered unconditionally when an allocation exists, route ungated in app_router.dart:2602-2607 (no SchoolBuildScope entry), so real parents see it. HOWEVER the 'dead button' half of the claim is false: AksharaInsightCard delegates to AksharaAiSuggestionBar, whose line 115 renders the action button only if (actionLabel != null && onAction != null) — with onAction null, NO 'Refresh ETA' button appears at all. Also the message itself contains an explicit (if jargony) disclaimer that live tracking is not enabled, though maxLines:2 + ellipsis (bar lines 109-110) may truncate that disclaimer on narrow phones.

### V-39 · CONFIRMED **P1** — Student home dashboard is seed-only: on real tenants it renders empty/contradictory data and loses all quick actions
- **Module:** student_app/dashboard · **Platform:** all-mobile · **Role:** Student · **Reported by:** journey-student
- **Original evidence:** student_handlers.ts:8-9 (dashboard = plain handleSnapshot('snapshot_dashboard'), NO overlay — unlike attendance:12-13 and timetable:24-26 which overlay real records); buildDefaultStudentSnapshot returns todaySchedule:[], attendanceKpi '--'/'No attendance recorded yet', homeworkDue:[], blank examReminder, quickActions:[], blank aiInsight (pilot_operations_repository.ts:1431-1449); only writers of snapshot_dashboard are migration demo seeds (supabase/migrations/20260614400000_mobile_read_apis.sql:104,135,164) and QA script scripts/qa/live_cert_pilot_full_year.py; teacher homework creation writes only homework_item entities, never the dashboard snapshot (pilot_operations_repository.ts:2382-2406); client renders everything from this payload including server-driven quickActions (student_dashboard_screen.dart:104-206, student_mapper.dart:28)
- **Original impact:** A real student's home screen shows no schedule, '--' attendance even when the Attendance tab shows real records, 0 homework due while the Learn tab lists real homework, and the 'Submit HW'/'Report Card'/'Progress' shortcuts vanish entirely. The flagship screen contradicts its own tabs; on the demo tenant it shows frozen seed data ('Marked 9:12 AM', exam dated 12 Jun 2026) forever.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Compute the dashboard server-side from the same live sources the tabs use (attendance records, homework_item entities, published exam schedule), and make quick actions client-defined navigation, not server snapshot content.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The core technical claim is fully accurate: the student home dashboard has no real-data overlay, its snapshot is written only by demo seeds/QA scripts, and on any real tenant every real student sees an empty schedule, '--' attendance with the false statement 'No attendance recorded yet' (even when the Attendance tab shows real records), HW-due 0 (even when the Learn tab lists real homework), and zero quick actions — all confirmed line-by-line. This is a genuine, user-visible data-contradiction defect on the flagship student screen, and the existence of the TEACH-5 teacher-dashboard overlay proves the team itself considers this defect class must-fix. However, P0 (production-blocking) is inflated: nothing crashes, no data is corrupted, no security/RBAC issue exists, every piece of real information is one tap away on tabs the dashboard itself links to, the real-data Adaptive AI feed on the same screen surfaces genuine homework/attendance nudges, and the empty state is a deliberately honest default rather than fabricated data. Additionally, the reviewer's 'frozen demo seed shown forever' evidence is factually wrong (those strings are client mock-mode only). A degraded-but-navigable home screen with misleading-empty KPIs is a major pre-GA quality gap for the student role — P1 (must fix before student-app GA), not a production-blocking P0.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** Verified at cited locations: (1) supabase/functions/_shared/student/student_handlers.ts:8-9 — handleDashboard is a plain handleSnapshot('snapshot_dashboard'); attendance (12-13) and timetable (24-26) use dedicated handlers. In mobile_read_handlers.ts:596-658 the student handleSnapshot only overlays snapshot_exams and snapshot_homework; snapshot_dashboard falls through to `return resolved` with NO real-data overlay, and on SnapshotNotFoundError resolveStudentSnapshot (559-580) returns buildDefaultStudentSnapshot. (2) pilot_operations_repository.ts:1431-1449 — default dashboard is exactly as claimed: todaySchedule:[], attendanceKpi value '--' / 'No attendance recorded yet', homeworkDue:[], blank examReminder, quickActions:[], blank aiInsight. (3) Writers: repo-wide grep confirms the only writers of student snapshot_dashboard are migration seed 20260614400000_mobile_read_apis.sql:164 (demo tenant) and scripts/qa/live_cert_pilot_full_year.py:257-263 (QA-only, minimal payload). Teacher homework creation (pilot_operations_repository.ts:2382-2406) inserts only homework_item entities — never touches the dashboard snapshot. (4) Client: student_dashboard_screen.dart:104-206 renders todaySchedule, quickActions (row is empty when server list is empty — no client fallback), attendance KPI, and HW-due count (lines 40-43 computed from data.homeworkDue, i.e. from the same stale snapshot, NOT from the real homework list provider); student_mapper.dart toDashboard maps quickActions ?? [] straight from payload. studentDashboardEmptyProvider is manual (default false), so no empty-state guard hides this. Contradiction is real: the attendance tab handler overlays real attendance records and the homework tab overlays real submissions, so tabs show real data while home shows '--'/0. TWO CORRECTIONS: (a) the 'Marked 9:12 AM' / '12 Jun 2026' frozen-demo claim is misattributed — those strings live in StudentDashboardData.mock() (student_dashboard_provider.dart:137-233), used only by mock_student_repository.dart and test fixtures, never by real API mode; the actual server seed at migration line 164 contains NO quickActions/todaySchedule/attendanceKpi/homeworkDue at all, so even the demo tenant shows the sparse screen (this strengthens the emptiness claim but the specific 'frozen seed data forever' evidence is wrong). (b) Mitigations the reviewer underweighted: the same screen embeds AdaptivePriorityFeedSection (W2 feed) which surfaces real own-data homework/attendance nudges, and the attendance/HW KPI cards still navigate to the real tabs; also the codebase already fixed this exact pattern for teachers (overlayTeacherDashboard, TEACH-5, line 3034-3049), confirming the team treats it as a real defect class — but also that the student side was consciously left with an honest-empty default ('No attendance recorded yet') rather than fabricated data.

### V-40 · CONFIRMED **P0** — No student login path exists in either client despite full backend support — the persona's access story is incoherent
- **Module:** auth · **Platform:** all · **Role:** Student · **Reported by:** journey-student
- **Original evidence:** Backend supports student_id-type OTP login yielding scope='student' (auth_handlers.ts:217-238, 372-387) and session scope-switching (auth_handlers.ts:571-609); the Flutter app only ever sends identifierType:'mobile' with no scope (auth_provider.dart:246-263, 282-301) and production role hardcoded to parent (login_screen.dart:52); no scope-switch call exists anywhere in lib/ (grep=0); web auth pages have no student entry (web/src/pages/auth/LoginPage.tsx); route guards wall the student shell to student-scope sessions only (app_router.dart:2471)
- **Original impact:** A Class 9 student with no phone (the designed persona: OTP goes to the parent) has no discoverable way to reach the 9-screen student app. If the parent's number maps to both parent and student memberships, the client offers no chooser — the server silently picks. The entire student product is effectively unreachable through the front door.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Add a 'Student? Sign in with your Student ID' path (school code + Public Student ID -> OTP to registered parent phone), and/or a parent-side 'Open student mode' using the existing scope-switch endpoint.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The core claim survives every refutation attempt: there is no production path in either client for a student to reach the student shell despite complete backend support (student_id OTP login, scope='student' issuance, and context-switch endpoint all implemented and unused by any client). The QA/demo student persona is not a mitigation because demo auth is disabled in production; deep links are blocked by the role guard; the parent shell is a different persona's product. The 'server silently picks' claim is structurally accurate (ordered scope fallback, no client chooser). Severity is honest, not inflated: an entire persona's 9-screen product being unreachable through any front door is production-blocking for that persona, consistent with this project's PRA standard that runtime unreachability of a built module is a real P0 gap and prior certifications are not evidence of reachability. Only defects found in the finding were two minor file-path inaccuracies in citations, which do not weaken the evidence.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** All cited evidence verified at the actual locations. Backend (supabase/functions/_shared/auth_handlers.ts): lines 217-238 accept type='student_id' in handleLogin and route OTP via resolveStudentLoginTargetWithClient; lines 372-387 set loginScope='student' on OTP verify; lines 560-618 implement handleContextSwitch (scope switching with token reissue). auth_context.ts:73-85 confirms silent scope fallback ordering when the client sends no scope (no chooser). Flutter client: lib/features/auth/auth_provider.dart lines 246-263 and 282-301 hardcode identifierType:'mobile' with no scope/schoolId/studentId; login_screen.dart hardcodes role=UserRole.parent when demo auth is off; grep of lib/ shows zero context-switch/scope-switch calls and zero student_id auth usage (only SIS data mappers); the only student entry is qa_login_persona.dart, which is demo/QA-only and disabled in production. Web: web/src/pages/auth/ contains only LoginPage.tsx and OtpVerificationPage.tsx with zero 'student' matches. Router guard (actual file lib/router/app_router.dart, _canAccessRoute ~line 2468): '/student' routes require UserRole.student, which a production session can never obtain. Two trivial citation-path errors (auth_provider.dart is not under providers/; app_router.dart is lib/router/ not lib/core/router/) — substance unaffected.

### V-41 · CONFIRMED **P0** — Student exam schedule can never show really-scheduled exams; 'Class average' KPI is seed-frozen
- **Module:** student_app/exams · **Platform:** all-mobile · **Role:** Student · **Reported by:** journey-student
- **Original evidence:** handleExams = handleSnapshot('snapshot_exams') (student_handlers.ts:20-22); overlayExamsSnapshotFromResults overlays ONLY examResults, returning seed upcomingExams/averagePercent/subjectScores untouched (pilot_operations_repository.ts:1884-1900); default fallback = averagePercent:0, upcomingExams:[] (1458-1464); grep shows no production writer of upcomingExams anywhere; real exam scheduling + hall tickets exist in the staff exam-administration module but never publish to student snapshots; UI renders the seed values as 'Upcoming' count and 'Class average' (student_exams_screen.dart:89-102, 131-133)
- **Original impact:** Exams are the product's top-priority module, yet when a school schedules a mid-term, students see nothing (or stale seed data); the first KPI on the Exams screen reads 'Class average 0%' on real tenants.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Publish scheduled exams from the exam-administration module into the student/parent read path, and compute average from published results (or drop the KPI).
- **Verifier reasoning:** I attempted to refute via three routes and all failed. (a) Hidden writer: none — exhaustive grep shows no production path ever writes upcomingExams or averagePercent into snapshot_exams; only demo-org migration seeds (themselves empty) and the zeroed fallback. (b) Alternate route that solves the task: the EXM-7 datesheet backend explicitly built 'so students/parents can see upcoming exams' exists, but it is gated on the staff 'viewExams' permission and is only consumed by the staff exam-admin reports screen — the student and parent apps have no path to it, which actually strengthens the finding (backend capability exists, student surface never wired). (c) Severity inflation: the Results half of the screen genuinely works (real published results are overlaid), so the screen is not fully dead. However, two things keep this at P0 under this project's own conventions: the 'Class average' KPI displays a fabricated 0% as fact even when real results exist (misleading data presented to end users), and the Upcoming feature — in the product's declared top-priority module (Exams) — can never function on any real tenant; a school scheduling a mid-term produces zero student-visible signal on the Exams screen. This is precisely the 'seed-frozen snapshot / fake data inside a certified live path' pattern the PRA program classifies as P0. Not a backend-only issue: the broken values are the first KPIs rendered on the student Exams screen. Confirmed at P0.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** Every cited location checks out. (1) student_handlers.ts:20-22 — handleExams delegates to handleSnapshot('snapshot_exams'); parent_handlers.ts:29 does the same. (2) pilot_operations_repository.ts:1458-1465 — default fallback snapshot is exactly {averagePercent:0, upcomingExams:[], examResults:[], subjectScores:[]}. (3) overlayExamsSnapshotFromResults (~1830-1901) overlays ONLY childName/childClass/schoolName + examResults from listPublishedResultsForStudent; it never touches upcomingExams, averagePercent, or subjectScores, so those remain seed/fallback values forever. (4) Writer search: repo-wide grep finds only two student_entities write paths in pilot_operations_repository.ts (line 812 homework review write-back, line 2384 homework_item fan-out) — both entity_type='homework_item'. The only snapshot_exams rows ever written are demo seeds in migration 20260614400000_mobile_read_apis.sql:110-169 for the 'default' org, and even those seed upcomingExams:[]. No production writer of upcomingExams/averagePercent exists anywhere. (5) Real scheduling DOES exist staff-side: scheduleExamSession (exam_administration_repository.ts:445) and the EXM-7 datesheet (loadDatesheet:1447, explicitly commented 'so students/parents can see upcoming exams') — but the datesheet route requires staff 'viewExams' permission and its only Flutter consumers are lib/features/academics/exam_admin/exam_reports_{provider,screen}.dart (staff exam-admin reports). Nothing in the student/parent app calls it. (6) student_exams_screen.dart:89-102 renders averagePercent as the 'Class average' KPI and upcomingExams.length as 'Upcoming'; line 131-133 renders the Upcoming tab from data.upcomingExams. So on a real tenant: Upcoming tab permanently empty, 'Class average 0%' shown even when real published results exist (overlay adds examResults but never recomputes averagePercent).

### V-42 · CONFIRMED **P0** — Web student portal is structurally dead: 6 of 9 pages can never render data, and homework cannot be submitted on web
- **Module:** web/student · **Platform:** web · **Role:** Student · **Reported by:** journey-student
- **Original evidence:** StudentDashboardPage + StudentProgressPage expect {kpis:[...]} via KpiPage (KpiPage.tsx:50) from /student/dashboard, which returns a snapshot with no kpis key; Attendance/Exams/ReportCard/Timetable use fetchList (list.ts:19-25 falls back to {items:[]}) against snapshot-shaped responses ({kpi,calendarDays,recentLogs}, {averagePercent,upcomingExams,...}, {days:[...]}) — StudentPages.tsx:30-39, 53-64, 66-77, 106-116; homework page is a read-only table with no submit action (StudentPages.tsx:41-51) while Flutter has a full submit sheet; profile page renders String(v) over Object.entries so the parentContacts array prints '[object Object]' (StudentPages.tsx:134-138 vs payload at pilot_operations_repository.ts:1370-1394)
- **Original impact:** A student on a desktop (the only web option) gets permanent 'loads once connected' placeholders on dashboard, attendance, timetable, exams, report card, and progress, and cannot submit homework at all. The claimed 1:1 parity with the Flutter app does not exist for this persona.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Add snapshot adapters per endpoint (or a shared student view-model API), and implement web homework submission against POST /student/homework/submit.
- **Verifier reasoning:** Attempted refutation failed on all fronts. The client-vs-backend contract mismatch is structural, not conditional: no amount of real data (attendance records, exam results, timetable slots) can ever render on 6 of the 9 student web pages, because the client parses shapes the student endpoints never return. There is no guard, alternate route, or fallback that mitigates it — router.tsx maps exactly these components, and the fetchList/KpiPage fallbacks actively hide the defect as an innocuous empty state (which is precisely why the 229-route live cert, which checked for crash/blank/401, missed it). Homework submission is a hard capability gap on web (backend route and Flutter flow exist; web has no action). Severity is honest at P0 for the web platform: the Student role — one of the personas the web app exists to serve under the stated 1:1 Flutter-parity mandate — cannot complete a single data-bearing task or the sole student mutation. This is a whole-persona functional failure shipped behind a misleading 'empty' UI, the definition of production-blocking for the web lane; the existence of the mobile app is a channel alternative, not a mitigation for the web product's own claim of parity. The only reviewer inaccuracy found is cosmetic (which placeholder copy shows when connected), which does not reduce severity.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** Every cited location checked and holds. (1) KpiPage.tsx:42-50 fetches endpoint and treats data as empty unless d.kpis is a non-empty array; StudentDashboardPage/StudentProgressPage (StudentPages.tsx:20-28) both point at /student/dashboard, whose handler (student_handlers.ts:8-10 → mobile_read_handlers.ts handleSnapshot) returns buildDefaultStudentSnapshot('snapshot_dashboard') (pilot_operations_repository.ts:1431-1449) — keys are greetingHeadline/todaySchedule/attendanceKpi/homeworkDue/etc., no 'kpis' key exists anywhere in the student snapshot path, so both pages permanently render the empty state regardless of data. (2) list.ts:19-25 only extracts Array from raw/items/data/key (no key passed by any student page) and silently falls back to {items:[],total:0}; the backend shapes are snapshot objects: attendance {kpi,calendarDays,recentLogs} (repo :1450-1457), exams/report-card {averagePercent,upcomingExams,examResults,subjectScores} (:1458-1465), timetable {days:[]...} (:1466-1474) — apiFetch unwraps the {data} envelope to the snapshot object (client.ts:92), which contains no items array, so Attendance/Exams/ReportCard/Timetable (StudentPages.tsx:30-39, 53-64, 66-77, 106-116) discard ALL real data and show EmptyState('Nothing to show yet') forever. The overlay functions (overlayAttendanceSnapshotFromRecords, overlayExamsSnapshotFromResults, overlayTimetableSnapshotFromSlots) preserve the snapshot shape, so real records never reach the web UI. (3) Homework and Notices DO render (student handleList → listEnvelope {items,...}, matching fetchList), consistent with the reviewer's '6 of 9' count (dashboard, progress, attendance, exams, report-card, timetable dead; homework-read, notices, profile alive). (4) Web homework page (StudentPages.tsx:41-51) is a read-only ResourceList with no mutation; backend POST /student/homework/submit exists (pilot_operations_router.ts:53) and Flutter wires it (lib/features/student_app/student_mutations_provider.dart:49-63 submitHomework) — so homework submission is genuinely impossible on web only. (5) Profile page maps Object.entries with String(v) (StudentPages.tsx:135-136) over a payload whose parentContacts is an array of objects and academicSummary an array (repo :1475-1485) → '[object Object]' rendering, confirmed. Router.tsx:346-354 confirms these are the ONLY student routes — no alternate web screen mitigates any of this. One wording nit: with a working backend the dead pages show 'Nothing to show yet' (AsyncBoundary empty branch), not the 'loads once connected' text (that copy appears only when the query is 'unavailable'); either way the pages are permanently dataless.

### V-43 · CONFIRMED **P1** — Entire web finance module is read-only — zero business mutations exist in the web app
- **Module:** finance · **Platform:** web · **Role:** accountant · **Reported by:** journey-accountant
- **Original evidence:** grep for POST across web/src: only web/src/lib/auth/AuthContext.tsx:115,127 (login/verify-otp). No finance page contains any write call. Dead buttons: CollectionsPage.tsx:46 ('Collect fee', no onClick), FeeStructuresPage.tsx:31 ('New structure'), DiscountsPage.tsx:30 ('Assign discount'), FinanceExtraPages.tsx:30 ('Assign fee'), :45 ('Record payment'), :58 ('New QR'). Backend supports all of these (finance_router.ts:164,221,288,310,320,341).
- **Original impact:** The accountant's primary 9-5 tool cannot perform one single money action. Every daily workflow — collect, refund, concession, resolve, close day — is blocked. Would fail instantly in a sales demo the moment a prospect clicks any button.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Treat web finance as unbuilt, not frozen. Implement the collect-fee flow first (student search → outstanding → amount → mode → receipt), then refunds, then offline resolution, wired to the already-certified endpoints.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The factual core is fully true — the web finance module has zero mutations and six visible dead buttons while the backend supports every action. However, P0 'production-blocking' overstates it: per recorded product decisions the product is mobile-first and the Flutter app (which carries the finance mutations) is the accountant's primary and the certified production tool; the web app is an owner-frozen parity-recreation lane whose live cert covered route rendering only, i.e., a known pre-GA scope gap rather than a regression blocking any live workflow. No business workflow is blocked product-wide. Dead affordances that silently do nothing are still a genuine UX defect and a hard blocker for any web GA or sales demo of the web surface, so this is a confirmed P1 (must fix before web GA), not P0.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** Every cited item verified: (1) grep across web/src shows the only non-GET calls are AuthContext.tsx:115,127 (POST /auth/login, /auth/verify-otp) — zero write calls anywhere in the web app; (2) all six cited buttons exist and have no onClick handler (CollectionsPage.tsx:46 'Collect fee', FeeStructuresPage.tsx:31 'New structure', DiscountsPage.tsx:30 'Assign discount', FinanceExtraPages.tsx 'Assign fee'/'Record payment'/'New QR'), and the Button component supplies no default action; only navigation onClicks exist (FinanceDashboardPage.tsx:43,46,112); (3) backend router supabase/functions/_shared/finance/finance_router.ts confirms POST handlers for collections, fee-structures, refunds (+approve/reject), discounts, offline payments (+reconcile/bounce), and QR sessions. Additional context found: the pages' own empty-state copy says data loads 'once the backend is connected', indicating the web finance pages were built as deliberate read-only shells.

### V-44 · CONFIRMED **P1** — No fee-collection flow on web: 'Collect fee' buttons dead or circular
- **Module:** finance/collections · **Platform:** web · **Role:** accountant · **Reported by:** journey-accountant
- **Original evidence:** CollectionsPage.tsx:46 <Button icon="add">Collect fee</Button> has no onClick. FinanceDashboardPage.tsx:46-48 'Collect fee' navigates to /finance/collections — the read-only list itself. Backend POST /finance/collections exists with idempotency (finance_router.ts:221-223); Flutter has the full dialog (finance_workflow_actions.dart:720-811).
- **Original impact:** The counter journey (200 transactions/day in peak season) cannot even START on web. Click count to collect a payment: infinite.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Row-level and global 'Collect fee' opening a drawer seeded from the student account balance, exactly as the Flutter dialog does; keyboard-first (autofocus search, Enter to submit).
- **Verifier reasoning:** The core claim survives fully — the web app cannot start a fee collection, the button is dead, the dashboard CTA is circular, and no mitigation route exists anywhere in web/src. However, P0 'production-blocking' is inflated on two grounds. First, the production counter journey does not run on web: the live pilot runs the Flutter app, which has the complete collection dialog wired to the working backend POST; the web app is deployed only as the /review/ UX demo on an isolated test tenant, and the web lane is owner-frozen after a route-render cert. So the claimed 200-transactions/day journey is not actually blocked in production today — 'click count: infinite' describes the web demo, not the deployed product. Second, the web track's certified state (229/229 routes) was accepted by the owner with the app effectively read-only, so this is a parity/completeness gap in a frozen lane, not a regression in a shipped money path. It still merits P1, not P2: the web track's charter is 100% Flutter parity, this is the flagship finance journey, the gap is absent from the track's own canonical gap register (unlike every other honest-state gap, WEB-001..009), and the inert 'Collect fee' button breaks the track's explicit honest-state rule by advertising a capability that silently does nothing — a genuine UX defect that must be fixed (or the button removed/disabled with a gap entry) before the web lane unfreezes toward production use by accountants.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** Every citation holds. (1) /Users/surendrakanna/Documents/Akshara_ERP/web/src/pages/finance/CollectionsPage.tsx:46 is exactly `actions={<Button icon="add">Collect fee</Button>}` — no onClick, an inert button. (2) FinanceDashboardPage.tsx:46-48 `<Button icon="add" onClick={() => navigate('/finance/collections')}>Collect fee</Button>` — navigates to the read-only list containing the dead button, so the loop is real. (3) Backend POST exists: supabase/functions/_shared/finance/finance_router.ts:221-223 routes `POST /finance/collections` to handleCreateCollection (note: the file is at _shared/finance/, not _shared/ as cited — trivial path drift). (4) lib/features/finance/finance_workflow_actions.dart:720 defines showRecordCollectionDialog — the full Flutter flow exists. (5) I searched all of web/src for mutations: the ONLY POST calls in the entire web app are /auth/login and /auth/verify-otp (AuthContext.tsx:115,127). There is no alternate route, drawer, or student-account action that records a payment — the web app is 100% read-only outside auth. (6) The gap is NOT tracked: web/VERIFIED_GAPS.md and docs/roadmap/WEB_TRACK_BACKEND_GAPS.md list WEB-001..009 with none covering collection writes; line 67 even confirms the backend write endpoints exist and calls the 9 core finance pages 'wired'. The dead button also violates the web track's own stated rule ('disable only the blocked interaction — honest state').

### V-45 · CONFIRMED **P1** — Student Accounts page calls a nonexistent endpoint and rows are dead — 'find student fast' impossible on web
- **Module:** finance/student-accounts · **Platform:** web · **Role:** accountant · **Reported by:** journey-accountant
- **Original evidence:** StudentAccountsPage.tsx:8 fetches GET /finance/student-accounts (list). finance_router.ts has only /finance/student-accounts/{id} (213-216) and /{id}/ledger (207-211) — no list route, so live returns 404 → ErrorState. The page also defines no onRowClick (whole file, lines 35-51) and no collect/ledger action. Flutter builds this list by composing fee-assignments + per-student fetches (api_finance_repository.dart:88-104).
- **Original impact:** The primary student-lookup surface for counter work errors out on live; even if it loaded, a found student is a dead end — no ledger view, no outstanding drill-down, no collect action. The parent-at-the-counter journey dies at step 1.
- **Reviewer recommendation:** Add the list endpoint (or compose like Flutter), make rows open a student fee ledger (backend /ledger already exists and is 'printable'), with a prominent Collect action.
- **Verifier reasoning:** The finding is factually correct end-to-end: the web Student Accounts page 404s on live and, even if fixed, rows lead nowhere — no ledger, no collect. However P0 'production-blocking' inflates impact. The product is mobile-first; the production pilot runs the Flutter app, where this journey works (client-side composition). The web lane is a frozen parity track whose live exposure is a demo/review tenant; its prior live cert checked route render (no crash/blank/401), which an ErrorState passes, explaining how this survived. So the parent-at-the-counter journey does not die in production today — it dies only on the web platform, which is not the primary counter surface. Real, must-fix before any web finance unfreeze/GA: P1, not P0.
- **What the verifier found at the cited locations:** All four evidence claims verified at the cited locations. (1) StudentAccountsPage.tsx:8 fetches GET /finance/student-accounts with pageSize 200. (2) finance_router.ts defines only /finance/student-accounts/{id}/ledger (line 207) and /finance/student-accounts/{id} (line 213); no list route among ~45 finance routes, and routeFinance returns a 404 errorEnvelope for any unmatched /finance/* path. (3) In live mode (VITE_DATA_MODE=live) useModuleQuery runs the query (unavailable only when IS_DEMO), and AsyncBoundary maps a 404 to ErrorState (only 403 MODULE_DISABLED and 422 get graceful states) — so the page errors on live, confirmed not mitigated by the 'awaiting backend' placeholder. (4) StudentAccountsPage passes no onRowClick and no actions (sibling CollectionsPage uses onRowClick, proving omission not limitation); no web code calls the existing /{id}/ledger endpoint; CollectionsPage's 'Collect fee' button also has no handler. Flutter contrast confirmed at lib/core/repositories/api/finance/api_finance_repository.dart ~87-110 (reviewer cited a slightly wrong path, lib/data/, but content matches: list composed from fee-assignments + per-student fetches).

## PART 2 — COMPLETE FINDING REGISTER (all 250, by reviewer, verbatim)

## Reviewer: Principal Product Designer — Design Systems & Visual Craft  (`principal-product-designer`)
**Vote:** CERTIFIED — moderate improvements required · **Scores:** overall 6.5, visualDesign 6.5, maturity 7, mobile 6, web 7

**Independent executive assessment:** Akshara's design-system engineering is far above the ERP norm: a real primitives→semantic→ColorScheme token pipeline with white-label overrides (lib/theme/color_tokens.dart), zero hardcoded hex colors across all 297 feature screens, 264/297 screens consuming the shared widget library, WCAG-anchored accessibility tokens, and a 70-image golden suite across three viewports with genuinely designed (not inverted) dark surfaces. The flagship dashboards prove it on screen: the finance dashboard adapts honestly from a 2-column phone grid to a 3-column tablet layout with chart, legend, and right rail (finance_dashboard_390x844 vs 834x1194 goldens) — this is credible modern-SaaS layout craft.

But the product currently ships THREE stacked visual identities — M15 academic blue, "Premium School OS" indigo→violet gradients, and four per-persona "Stitch" palettes — and the Stitch layer is hard-forced by every persona shell, silently overriding the user's appearance setting (teachers/students/admins locked to dark, parents locked to light) and breaking brand parity with the web app, which mirrors only M15. Add one genuine dead-end in the parent's primary navigation (the "PTM" More-sheet tile routes to an Access Denied screen), an intelligence dashboard whose fixed-width cards render as a half-empty broken column on phones, and a declared Roboto/RobotoMono type identity that is never bundled (so iOS renders a different typeface and the mono receipt style silently degrades). The foundation deserves an 8; the un-governed top layer is what a demo audience would actually see. Moderate, well-scoped fixes — one theme authority, responsive KPI cards, shipped fonts, and pruning the dead nav tile — would move this from "impressively engineered" to genuinely world-class.

**Strengths observed (context for what must not regress):**
- Token architecture is top-decile: primitives → semantic tokens → M3 ColorScheme + ThemeExtensions, with white-label primary override deriving container pairs (lib/theme/color_tokens.dart:242-254) — and the web mirrors the exact same values as CSS variables (web/src/index.css:31-67).
- Perfect color discipline in screens: zero Color(0x...) literals across all 297 *_screen.dart files; only ~28 raw Colors.* uses in the entire feature tree; semantic status chips resolved through tokens (lib/shared/semantic_status.dart).
- Dark mode is designed, not inverted: obsidian layered surfaces, documented contrast fixes with a rendered-contrast audit test (color_tokens.dart:209-215), confirmed by dark goldens (dark_finance_dashboard_834x1194.png, parent_dashboard_dark_390x844.png).
- Flagship responsive craft is real: finance dashboard restructures phone→tablet (2-col grid → 3-col + chart/legend + right rail + CTA card), verified in rendered goldens, not just code.
- PersonaNav is a model shared component: test-enforced max 4 primary tabs + More sheet, workspace switcher surfaced for multi-hat users, selected-index logic centralized (lib/shared/navigation/persona_nav.dart:58-70).
- Web UI primitives are disciplined 1:1 ports of the Flutter widgets (e.g. KpiCard.tsx documents 'Ports AksharaKpiCard', uses only token utilities: bg-primary-container, p-s5, shadow-ak-2), plus a CommandPalette in the shell — modern SaaS affordances.
- Accessibility is tokenized: WCAG ratio helpers, 11px label floor, exactly one sanctioned text-scale clamp for dense grids (lib/theme/accessibility.dart:1-40).
- Hide-first scope control is executed correctly and reversibly: SchoolBuildScope centrally hides salon/restaurant/healthcare/accommodation/white-label from nav AND blocks their routes (lib/core/config/school_build_scope.dart:22-99, route_guards.dart:411).
- 70 golden screenshots across 3 viewports including dark variants — visual regression discipline most ERP vendors never build.

**Findings:**

### F-001 · [P0] · **verified: P1** (see Part 1 V-01) — Parent 'PTM' nav tile dead-ends on an Access Denied screen
- **Platform:** all-mobile · **Module:** parent / navigation · **Role:** parent
- **Evidence:** lib/features/parent/shell/parent_shell.dart:95-96 puts route RouteNames.parentPtm ('/parent/ptm', route_names.dart:33) in the More sheet; lib/shared/navigation/persona_nav.dart:197-216 renders spec.more with NO SchoolBuildScope filter; '/parent/ptm' is in hiddenRoutePrefixes (lib/core/config/school_build_scope.dart:76-77); lib/router/app_router.dart:2610-2614 returns AccessDeniedScreen for it. Trace: parent taps More → PTM tile → Access Denied.
- **Impact:** A first-class tile in the parent app's own navigation leads to a refusal screen. Parents are the largest user population; this looks broken in every demo and erodes trust in the whole More sheet.
- **Recommendation:** Filter MoreNavDestination entries through SchoolBuildScope.isRouteHidden (and the backend-surface gate) inside MoreNavSheet/PersonaNavSpec so gated features never render an entry point; re-add the tile when the PTM backend ships.

### F-002 · [P1] — Persona shells hard-force theme + brightness; the Appearance setting is a no-op in the entire app
- **Platform:** all-mobile · **Module:** theme / settings · **Role:** all
- **Evidence:** lib/app/app.dart:67-75 wires themeModeProvider into MaterialApp (light/dark M15), and an Appearance Settings screen exists (app_router.dart:236-239; golden dark_appearance_settings_390x844.png). But all four persona ShellRoutes (app_router.dart:257,432,558,2291) wrap their subtree in Theme(data: AksharaAppTheme.stitch(...)) — admin_shell.dart:36-42, parent_shell.dart:118-122, teacher_shell.dart:86, student_shell.dart:80 — whose brightness is fixed per palette (stitch_palettes.dart:30-35: parent=light, everyone else=dark).
- **Impact:** Teachers, students and admins are locked into permanent dark mode (poor daylight-classroom legibility); parents can never get dark mode. The user-facing theme toggle changes only the settings screen itself, then appears broken the moment the user navigates back — a credibility-destroying 'setting that does nothing'.
- **Recommendation:** Make the persona palette respond to themeModeProvider: give each Stitch persona a light AND dark variant (or apply the persona hue over the M15 light/dark surfaces), and never override brightness against the user's explicit choice.

### F-003 · [P1] — Three stacked brand identities: M15 blue, Premium indigo→violet, and four Stitch persona primaries
- **Platform:** all-mobile · **Module:** theme / design-system · **Role:** all
- **Evidence:** Primary #1A56DB 'Premium Academic Blue' (color_tokens.dart:8-13); brand gradient #5B5BF0→#8B5CF6 'Premium School OS' (premium_tokens.dart:81-95); Stitch primaries #6366F1 admin / #0058BE parent / #4EDEA3 student / #8083FF teacher (stitch_palettes.dart:40,80,120,160). Visible in goldens: finance dashboard uses blue buttons + a purple gradient CTA card (finance_dashboard_834x1194.png), admin hub uses purple hero + teal gradient icon chips (dark_admin_hub_834x1194.png).
- **Impact:** There is no single answer to 'what color is Akshara?'. Screens mix blue controls, purple heroes and teal iconography; per-persona hues mean a multi-hat user's app changes identity when switching hats. This dilutes brand memory and reads as accumulated redesigns rather than one intentional system.
- **Recommendation:** Pick one brand authority (M15 blue with the gradient as an accent role is the least-cost path), fold the Stitch persona hues into accent tokens rather than replacement primaries, and document the decision in the theme layer.

### F-004 · [P1] — Flutter and web disagree on the product's visual identity for the same persona
- **Platform:** all · **Module:** theme / web parity · **Role:** admin
- **Evidence:** Web ships ONLY M15 light/dark with a working theme toggle (web/src/index.css:31-67 = #1A56DB; no Stitch values anywhere in web/src — grep for 6366F1/0058BE/4EDEA3/stitch returns nothing). The Flutter admin persona is forced dark-indigo obsidianEnterprise (admin_shell.dart:36-42, stitch_palettes.dart:40-75).
- **Impact:** The same school admin sees a light academic-blue product on desktop web and a forced-dark indigo product on mobile. For a platform explicitly built as a '1:1 parity recreation', the brand-level parity break is the first thing a customer comparing devices will notice.
- **Recommendation:** Whichever theme authority wins (see brand finding), apply it to both platforms; port the persona accent layer to web or remove it from mobile.

### F-005 · [P1] — Intelligence dashboard renders as a broken half-width column on phones and a ~70% empty page on tablets
- **Platform:** all-mobile · **Module:** intelligence · **Role:** principal/management
- **Evidence:** Golden intelligence_dashboard_390x844.png: all KPI cards form a single narrow left column (~165px) with the right half of the screen empty; intelligence_dashboard_834x1194.png: two rows of small cards occupy ~20% of the page, remainder blank. Cause: AksharaExecutiveKpiCard fixed width 168 (lib/shared/widgets/akshara_executive_kpi_card.dart:22,131-132) laid out in bare Wrap (lib/features/intelligence/management/intelligence_hub_screen.dart:131-167,201-214).
- **Impact:** An executive-facing analytics surface — exactly what gets shown in sales demos — looks unfinished/broken on every phone and barren on tablets, while sibling dashboards (finance, inventory) use proper responsive grids. Inconsistency makes it look like a defect, not a style.
- **Recommendation:** Reuse the FinanceKpiRow/responsive-grid pattern: cards expand to fill available columns per breakpoint; add a chart or trend section to give tablet pages a second content band.

### F-006 · [P1] — Declared type identity (Roboto / RobotoMono) is never bundled — iOS gets a different typeface and mono styles silently fail
- **Platform:** all-mobile · **Module:** theme / typography · **Role:** all
- **Evidence:** pubspec.yaml contains no fonts: section and no google_fonts dependency (grep 'fonts|google_fonts|Roboto' pubspec.yaml → no hits); lib/theme/typography.dart:53-54 declares robotoFamily='Roboto', robotoMonoFamily='RobotoMono'; monoBody is the specified style for IDs/receipts (typography.dart:5,360).
- **Impact:** Android happens to have Roboto as system font, iOS does not — so the two mobile platforms render different typefaces for the identical design system. RobotoMono exists on neither, so receipt numbers/IDs lose tabular alignment everywhere. The type scale's craft (weights, tracking) is real, but the family identity is an illusion.
- **Recommendation:** Bundle Roboto + RobotoMono (or adopt an intentional brand face like Inter) in pubspec assets, and add a golden that renders a receipt/ID string to lock the mono behavior.

### F-007 · [P1] — Detail screens on portrait tablet: center-floated narrow cards under full-width left headers
- **Platform:** tablet · **Module:** finance · **Role:** finance admin
- **Evidence:** Golden qa_x_027_finance_collection_detail_834x1194.png: 'Payment timeline'/'Installment history' section headers sit at the left margin while their content cards float centered at ~365px width, leaving large asymmetric gutters; screen: lib/features/finance/collection_detail/finance_collection_detail_screen.dart:109-172 (stretch column with card/table split at AdminLayout.useCardLayout, admin_layout.dart:27-31).
- **Impact:** The composition looks accidental — headers and content don't share an alignment grid, and half the tablet viewport is dead space on a daily finance workflow screen. Undermines the polish the flagship dashboards establish.
- **Recommendation:** On portrait tablet either stretch cards to the content column or constrain headers+content to one shared max-width column (like AdminLayout.constrainContent does on desktop).

### F-008 · [P1] — Visual QA goldens certify an appearance production users never see
- **Platform:** all-mobile · **Module:** design QA / golden suite · **Role:** all
- **Evidence:** teacher_dashboard_390x844.png and student_dashboard_390x844.png render in M15 light theme (test/golden/erp_dashboards_golden_test.dart pumps screens without persona shells), but in the shipped app those screens are always wrapped in forced dark Stitch themes (app_router.dart:432,2291 + teacher_shell.dart:86, student_shell.dart:80). parent_dashboard_dark_*.png goldens depict a state that is unreachable (parent shell forces light).
- **Impact:** The design system's principal safety net — 70 golden images — validates layouts against palettes users never see. Dark-on-Stitch contrast issues (e.g. studentPulse emerald on #171F33) can regress with zero golden coverage while the suite stays green.
- **Recommendation:** Pump persona-shell-wrapped screens (or at minimum apply the matching Stitch theme) in golden tests so the baseline equals production rendering.

### F-009 · [P2] — Chart series colors rotate hue between light and dark modes
- **Platform:** all · **Module:** theme / charts · **Role:** all
- **Evidence:** color_tokens.dart:233-237: dark chart1=chart2(light)=teal, chart2=indigo, chart4=blue — the light-mode series-1 blue becomes teal in dark. Visible: finance chart bars are blue in finance_dashboard_834x1194.png and teal in dark_finance_dashboard_834x1194.png.
- **Impact:** The same metric changes color identity when the OS theme flips; legends re-teach, screenshots across modes look like different data.
- **Recommendation:** Keep series hue stable across modes; adjust only lightness/saturation for dark backgrounds.

### F-010 · [P2] — Raw Material Colors used for risk semantics inside the intelligence module
- **Platform:** all-mobile · **Module:** intelligence · **Role:** principal/management
- **Evidence:** lib/features/intelligence/management/intelligence_hub_screen.dart:592-596: _colorForRisk returns Colors.red / Colors.orange / Colors.green instead of tokens (context.colors.error / warning / success). ~28 raw saturated Colors.* uses remain across lib/features (grep count).
- **Impact:** Saturated stock Material hues clash with the refined desaturated semantic palette and ignore dark-mode variants — visual dissonance exactly on high-attention risk indicators.
- **Recommendation:** Sweep the ~28 raw uses to semantic tokens; add a lint (or existing token test) banning Colors.red/green/orange in features.

### F-011 · [P2] — Sparse-permission Admin Hub leaves the tablet page half-empty with an abrupt canvas cutoff
- **Platform:** tablet · **Module:** admin hub / workspace switcher · **Role:** multi-hat staff
- **Evidence:** Golden workspace_switcher_multihat_834x1194.png (AdminHubScreen for teacher+inventory hats, test/golden/workspace_switcher_visual_test.dart:16-27): hero + chip row + a single module card end at ~46% page height; the canvas gradient terminates in a visible hard edge with pure white below.
- **Impact:** For narrow-permission or multi-hat users the hub reads as an unfinished page; the gradient seam looks like a rendering bug.
- **Recommendation:** Extend the canvas gradient to fill the viewport, and give sparse hubs a secondary band (recent activity, workspace shortcuts) or vertically center the content group.

### F-012 · [P2] — Out-of-scope verticals (salon/restaurant/healthcare) remain fully wired in nav config, routes, permissions and copilot copy
- **Platform:** all-mobile · **Module:** admin / scope hygiene · **Role:** super admin
- **Evidence:** kAllAdminNavDestinations still declares Salon/Restaurant/Healthcare/Accommodation/White Label tiles (lib/features/admin/admin_navigation_provider.dart:165-219); superAdmin still holds viewSalonBusiness etc. (lib/core/security/role_permissions.dart:164-173); copilot names 'Salon Dashboard' (lib/features/copilot/copilot_role_intelligence.dart:204-242). Currently hidden ONLY by the single const flag SchoolBuildScope.enabled + set membership (school_build_scope.dart:19-41), verified filtered at admin_navigation_provider.dart:230-232 and blocked at route_guards.dart:411.
- **Impact:** Hide-first is honored today (no defect reachable), but a one-line flag flip or a missed filter in any NEW nav surface re-exposes salon/restaurant inside a school ERP. The copilot string table already leaks the vocabulary.
- **Recommendation:** Given the owner decision is final ('never'), remove the vertical entries from nav config, role grants, and copilot strings even while keeping the screens in-repo; keep SchoolBuildScope for the experimental (not never-scope) modules.

### F-013 · [P2] — Admin hub tablet grid alignment is a near-miss: trailing gutter and hero/grid width mismatch
- **Platform:** tablet · **Module:** admin hub · **Role:** admin
- **Evidence:** dark_admin_hub_834x1194.png: purple hero spans full content width (~24→810) while the 3-column tile grid ends at ~715, leaving a ~95px empty right gutter on every row; tile rows also show uneven bottom whitespace versus the 2-tile last row.
- **Impact:** Subtle but persistent misalignment on the admin landing page — the first screen every staff demo opens — reads as unfinished against the otherwise strong hero card.
- **Recommendation:** Make the grid consume the same content width as the hero (4 columns at 834px, or stretch tiles), matching the finance dashboard's grid math.

---

## Reviewer: Senior UX Researcher — cognitive load, mental models, learnability  (`senior-ux-researcher`)
**Vote:** CERTIFIED — moderate improvements required · **Scores:** overall 6, workflowEfficiency 6, maturity 5, aiExperience 5

**Independent executive assessment:** Akshara's consumer personas (parent/teacher/student) are close to the "easiest school ERP" promise: a disciplined, test-enforced 4-tabs-plus-More contract, a real USER→ROLE→WORKSPACE→TASK scoping model that makes a finance clerk see only Finance, and genuinely strong daily workflows (teacher attendance with context carry-over and bulk actions; exam marks entry with focus-advance, AB/ML/DB statuses and a phase-gated approval lifecycle). The out-of-scope verticals (salon/restaurant/healthcare/white-label) are correctly hidden in the school build at both nav and route level.

However, the audit found one structural P0 that directly contradicts the product's own priorities: the flagship Exams module is missing from every workspace's module set, so the workspace-scoped Admin Hub card grid and the phone-only admin bottom nav — the primary surfaces of this "mobile-first" ERP — never show Exams at all; it is reachable on mobile only via buried in-page shortcuts, the exact state the "A5 un-bury" fix claimed to resolve (the fix only landed on the desktop rail). Around that sit maturity leaks a first-time user hits immediately: the production login screen tells parents "OTP is verified by the staging server" and its failure toast tells real users to "use a registered demo-school phone number"; the parent More menu offers PTM which dead-ends in a bare "Access Denied"; the default AI ball visually replaces the middle bottom-nav tab (Fees for parents, Teach for teachers, Schedule for students); and mobile/web use different vocabularies for the same teacher tasks ("Classes/Teach" vs "Attendance/Homework/Exams"). None of this requires redesign — the IA bones are good — but these defects sit on the first screen, the flagship module, and the money journey, so moderate fixes are required before wide rollout.

**Strengths observed (context for what must not regress):**
- Persona nav contract: at most 4 primary tabs + shared More sheet, single source of truth, enforced by test (lib/shared/navigation/persona_nav.dart:58-61) — prevents the tab-drift that plagues most ERPs
- Workspace model genuinely implements USER→ROLE→WORKSPACE→TASK: financeAdmin sees only the Finance module, admissionsCounselor sees Admissions+Marketing (lib/core/workspace/workspace.dart:95-112, 169-185)
- Hide-first is real: all non-school verticals + experimental surfaces are hidden from nav AND route-blocked in the school build (lib/core/config/school_build_scope.dart:22-41, 54-84; lib/router/route_guards.dart:411-421)
- Exam marks entry is a well-modeled workflow: per-row focus advance, Save-all batch, AB/ML/DB non-present statuses, phase-gated action bar with explanatory copy like 'Coordinator verification required before principal approval' (lib/features/academics/exam_admin/exam_marks_entry_screen.dart:419-497, 642)
- Teacher attendance carries context: tapping a today-period on Home pre-selects that class in the attendance screen, plus All-present/Fill-remaining bulk actions and a mark-all-before-submit guard (lib/features/teacher/attendance/teacher_attendance_screen.dart:34-35, 288-312, 387)
- Web sidebar IA is cleanly grouped (Overview/People/Academics/Operations/Finance/Engage/Configure) with no vertical clutter (web/src/routes/navConfig.ts:25-84)
- Honest no-data policy — empty states explicitly refuse to fabricate data instead of showing fake numbers (web/src/pages/intelligence/IntelligencePages.tsx:25,29)
- Old admin module placeholder screen is now dead code — no route dead-ends into it (only reference is a comment at lib/router/admin_navigation.dart:49)

**Findings:**

### F-014 · [P0] · **verified: P1** (see Part 1 V-02) — Exams module is invisible in the Admin Hub and mobile admin bottom nav — no workspace contains AdminModule.exams
- **Platform:** all · **Module:** exams / admin navigation · **Role:** principal, schoolAdmin, exam coordinator
- **Evidence:** lib/core/workspace/workspace.dart:67-85 — the schoolAdministration workspace's modules set lists 16 modules but omits AdminModule.exams (no other workspace contains it either). lib/features/admin/admin_navigation_provider.dart:272-284 — workspaceScopedNavDestinationsProvider filters nav to workspace.containsModule(). lib/core/workspace/workspace_providers.dart:24-34 — activeWorkspaceProvider always falls back to the user's first workspace, so the filter is always active for staff. Consumers: lib/features/admin/screens/admin_hub_screen.dart:28 (hub card grid, all form factors) and lib/features/admin/admin_bottom_nav.dart:47 (phone-only bottom nav). The Exams destination itself exists with an 'A5 — un-bury the flagship Exam Administration module' comment (admin_navigation_provider.dart:58-68) but only the desktop/tablet rail uses the unscoped provider (admin_navigation_rail.dart:56). Only remaining mobile entry points: lib/features/management/academics/management_academics_screen.dart:119 and lib/features/school_completion/school_completion_hub_screen.dart:25 — in-page shortcuts.
- **Impact:** On a mobile-first product whose stated top-priority module is Exams, a principal on a phone cannot find Exams in the bottom nav or the Admin Hub ('Jump to a module you are authorized to access' — and Exams is not there). On desktop the rail shows Exams while the hub grid on the same screen does not — contradictory same-screen navigation. This re-creates the exact burial the A5 fix documented, and would derail a sales demo of the flagship module.
- **Recommendation:** Add AdminModule.exams to the schoolAdministration workspace modules set (one line in workspace.dart), and add a regression test asserting every destination in kAllAdminNavDestinations that survives school-build filtering belongs to at least one workspace.

### F-015 · [P0] · **verified: P1** (see Part 1 V-03) — Production login copy leaks internal jargon: 'staging server' and 'demo-school phone number' shown to real parents
- **Platform:** all-mobile · **Module:** auth · **Role:** parent (first screen of every session)
- **Evidence:** lib/features/auth/login_screen.dart:156-159 — in the NON-demo branch the subtitle reads 'Enter your registered mobile number. OTP is verified by the staging server.' login_screen.dart:73-79 — the OTP-send failure SnackBar in the NON-demo branch reads 'Unable to send OTP. Use a registered demo-school phone number.' (the branches appear inverted: the demo branch gets the generic message).
- **Impact:** The very first screen a parent — or a prospect in a sales demo — sees announces a 'staging server'; a failed OTP tells a real production user their school is a 'demo-school'. Erodes trust instantly and reads as an unfinished product. Copy-only fix, but P0 by demo-embarrassment on the entry screen.
- **Recommendation:** Swap the two error strings and rewrite the production subtitle ('We'll send a one-time password to your registered mobile number'). Add a copy-lint rule banning 'staging', 'demo', 'mock', 'gap WEB-' in non-QA user-facing strings.

### F-016 · [P1] — Default AI ball visually replaces the middle bottom-nav tab (Fees / Teach / Schedule) in all three consumer shells
- **Platform:** all-mobile · **Module:** copilot / persona shells · **Role:** parent, teacher, student
- **Evidence:** Goldens: test/golden/goldens/persona_shell_student_390x844.png, persona_shell_parent_390x844.png, persona_shell_teacher_390x844.png — the 3rd of 5 nav slots shows NO tab icon; a 56px circular AI button sits exactly over it with the tab's label squeezed beneath. Code: lib/features/copilot/widgets/copilot_bottom_nav_ai_slot.dart:23-33 (Positioned top:-22, centered over the NavigationBar, rendered above it in the Stack — persona_nav.dart:86-119); lib/features/copilot/settings/ai_access_preferences_provider.dart:52-55 — bottomNavCenter is the DEFAULT for parent/teacher/student. Middle-slot tabs: parent = Fees (parent_shell.dart:46-55), teacher = Teach i.e. homework+exams (teacher_shell.dart:37-43), student = Schedule (student_shell.dart:38).
- **Impact:** The parent's money tab and the teacher's homework/exams tab lose their icon entirely and most of their tap target; tapping where the icon should be opens an AI chat instead. Users form the mental model 'the big center button is Fees', get an AI assistant, and lose trust in both the tab bar and the AI. Mitigated only by the small label strip below the ball and by Home-screen shortcuts.
- **Recommendation:** Either reduce primary tabs to 4 with a true empty center slot (dock pattern), or move the default AI entry to the app bar for consumer personas and let users opt into the center ball.

### F-017 · [P1] — Parent More menu offers 'PTM' which renders a bare 'Access Denied' screen
- **Platform:** all-mobile · **Module:** parent / navigation · **Role:** parent
- **Evidence:** lib/features/parent/shell/parent_shell.dart:94-98 — static MoreNavDestination 'PTM' → RouteNames.parentPtm. lib/core/config/school_build_scope.dart:75-77 — parentMeetings + parentPtm are in hiddenRoutePrefixes (PTM backend not shipped). lib/router/app_router.dart:2610-2615 — the route builder returns AccessDeniedScreen. lib/router/route_guards.dart:247-259 — that screen is just a lock icon + 'Access Denied', no explanation, no action. Additionally lib/router/parent_navigation.dart:86-87 routes hardcoded mock ids 'notice_n1'/'event_e2' to the same dead PTM route from dashboard taps.
- **Impact:** The app's own menu leads a parent to a dead end that sounds like their account is broken ('Access Denied' implies fault/permission problems, not an unavailable feature). Dead-end on a visible first-level menu item; classic trust-destroyer for non-technical parents.
- **Recommendation:** Filter More-sheet destinations through SchoolBuildScope (the same source of truth the router uses), and replace AccessDeniedScreen with two distinct states: 'not available at your school' vs 'you don't have permission', each with a way back.

### F-018 · [P1] — Web Intelligence nav ships two permanently-dead tabs ('Trust', 'AI Economics') whose empty states expose internal gap IDs and endpoint paths
- **Platform:** web · **Module:** intelligence · **Role:** principal, management
- **Evidence:** web/src/routes/navConfig.ts:155-156 — 'Trust' and 'AI Economics' are permanent tabs of the Intelligence module. web/src/pages/intelligence/IntelligencePages.tsx:24-29 — both render an unavailable state whose user-facing copy reads 'will load from /intelligence/trust once that endpoint is exposed (gap WEB-006)'. The file's own comment confirms the endpoints are pending.
- **Impact:** Two of seven Intelligence tabs can never show data, and the explanation shown to a principal is engineering backlog language ('gap WEB-006', raw endpoint paths). 'AI Economics' is also meaningless terminology for school leadership. Undermines the credibility of the entire Intelligence area.
- **Recommendation:** Remove the two tabs until the endpoints exist (feature-flag them like Flutter's surface_backend_gate does), and rewrite unavailable-state copy for humans.

### F-019 · [P1] — Overlapping staff concepts force guesswork: 'HR' vs 'Employee Platform', three executive surfaces, and five near-synonymous Configure entries on web
- **Platform:** all · **Module:** admin IA / naming · **Role:** schoolAdmin, clerk, principal
- **Evidence:** lib/features/admin/admin_navigation_provider.dart:69-84 — 'HR' (registry, attendance, leave, payroll) and 'Employee Platform' ('Employee 360 profiles, role assignment, workforce operations', lines 338-345) are separate top-level modules covering the same people. Exec overlap: 'Management', 'Director', 'Control Center' all present as leadership dashboards (lines 85-148), with Management itself containing Admissions/Finance/Academics sub-tabs that mirror the top-level modules (route_names.dart:341-376). Web: navConfig.ts:75-83 — Configure section = 'School Setup', 'Setup & Ops', 'Operations', 'Admin', 'Settings': five labels a clerk cannot tell apart.
- **Impact:** A clerk asked to 'update a teacher's role' cannot predict whether it lives in HR or Employee Platform; a principal cannot predict which of five Configure entries holds a given setting. This is the single biggest driver of training time on the admin side — every ambiguous pair roughly doubles search cost for novices.
- **Recommendation:** Merge Employee Platform into HR (or demote it to an HR tab named 'Employee 360'); collapse Configure to two entries (School Setup, System Settings); position Director/Control Center as chain-only so single-school staff never see three executive surfaces.

### F-020 · [P1] — Same persona, two vocabularies: mobile teacher tabs 'Classes'→attendance and 'Teach'→homework+exams vs web portal 'Attendance/Homework/Exams'
- **Platform:** all · **Module:** teacher / cross-platform naming · **Role:** teacher
- **Evidence:** lib/features/teacher/shell/teacher_shell.dart:30-43 — tab 'Classes' routes to RouteNames.teacherAttendance; tab 'Teach' routes to teacherHomework with exams as a matchPrefix (no visible 'Exams' or 'Attendance' label in mobile primary nav). web/src/routes/navConfig.ts:89-96 — the same teacher on web gets literal 'Attendance', 'Homework', 'Exams' items. Module level: Flutter top-level 'Exams' (admin_navigation_provider.dart:64) vs web 'Examinations' under 'Academics' (navConfig.ts:48-49); Flutter 'Student SIS' (line 53) vs web 'Students' (line 39).
- **Impact:** Exams — the top-priority feature — has no visible label in the mobile teacher nav; a teacher must learn that 'Teach' contains it. Schools training staff across phone and web must maintain two vocabularies; help material and word-of-mouth instructions ('open Attendance') fail on mobile.
- **Recommendation:** Adopt one task vocabulary everywhere: rename mobile tabs to 'Attendance' and 'Homework & Exams' (or split), and align web/Flutter module names (Exams, Students).

### F-021 · [P1] — Parent Home stacks four overlapping AI/aggregation surfaces plus the AI ball — indistinguishable value propositions
- **Platform:** all-mobile · **Module:** parent dashboard · **Role:** parent
- **Evidence:** lib/features/parent/dashboard/parent_dashboard_screen.dart:109-176 — one scroll contains: ParentReminderBanners (fees/PTM/forms), AdaptivePriorityFeedSection (AI feed), 'Action Needed' tile ('Fees, meetings, forms & homework that need you'), 'Parent Experience Hub' tile ('Homework status, exam readiness & structured insights'), 'Parent Insights' tile ('AI summaries of your child's progress'), and AksharaAiSuggestionBar — plus the bottom-nav AI ball (copilot_bottom_nav_ai_slot.dart). Reminder banners, adaptive feed and Action Needed all surface fees/homework/meetings.
- **Impact:** Three differently-named containers promise the same thing (what needs my attention) and two more promise AI summaries. A parent cannot form a model of where to look; the screen reads as long and repetitive, and the genuinely useful Action Inbox is diluted. 'Parent Experience Hub' is internal product language, not parent language.
- **Recommendation:** Collapse to one 'Needs your attention' surface (merge banners + adaptive feed + Action Inbox entry) and one insights entry; rename 'Parent Experience Hub' to plain language or fold it into Academics.

### F-022 · [P2] — Admin mobile bottom nav shows the first 4 modules by static declaration order — Marketing outranks SIS and Exams for a principal
- **Platform:** all-mobile · **Module:** admin navigation · **Role:** principal, schoolAdmin
- **Evidence:** lib/features/admin/admin_bottom_nav.dart:31,50 — visible tabs = destinations.take(4) of the workspace-scoped list, which follows kAllAdminNavDestinations declaration order: Admin Hub, Admissions, Marketing, Finance (admin_navigation_provider.dart:17-49). SIS, Exams (absent entirely per the P0), HR, Management all land behind 'More'.
- **Impact:** A principal's daily phone nav spends a prime slot on Marketing while student records and exams need an extra tap every time. Order encodes the build sequence, not user frequency.
- **Recommendation:** Define per-workspace tab order (e.g. schoolAdministration: Hub, SIS, Exams, Finance) instead of reusing the global declaration order.

### F-023 · [P2] — No first-run guidance anywhere despite a 14-module admin surface
- **Platform:** all · **Module:** onboarding / learnability · **Role:** schoolAdmin, clerk (new users)
- **Evidence:** grep across lib/ for first_run/walkthrough/coach mark/showcase/tour returns zero hits. The Admin Hub's only orientation is the subtitle 'Jump to a module you are authorized to access' (admin_hub_screen.dart:38-39). Existing onboarding features are data onboarding (invites/CSV imports — onboarding_hub_screen.dart), not user orientation. The dark_admin_hub_834x1194.png golden shows a wall of 14 near-identical module cards.
- **Impact:** Consumer personas are simple enough to self-teach, but a new school admin faces 14 modules with up to 14 sub-tabs each and no progressive disclosure of 'start here' (the Smart School Discovery wizard is buried under Management → Settings). Learnability rests entirely on external training.
- **Recommendation:** Add a one-time 'set up your school' checklist card to the Admin Hub (link the existing discovery wizard, invite flow, and first fee structure/exam) — no tour framework needed.

### F-024 · [P2] — Web nav gates Attendance, Alumni, Director and Intelligence on the 'management' module grant
- **Platform:** web · **Module:** web navigation / RBAC-UX · **Role:** attendance clerk, non-management staff
- **Evidence:** web/src/routes/navConfig.ts:31-32,42,56 — nav items 'Director', 'Intelligence', 'Alumni' and 'Attendance' all carry moduleKey 'management'; navForRole (lines 278-288) hides items whose moduleKey is not in the user's granted modules.
- **Impact:** A staff member granted attendance duties but not the management module loses the Attendance sidebar entry entirely; conversely anyone with management sees Director/Intelligence even when irrelevant. Coarse gating makes what-you-see unpredictable relative to what-you-do.
- **Recommendation:** Give attendance, alumni, director and intelligence their own module keys mirrored in the grants payload.

### F-025 · [P2] — Out-of-scope verticals (Salon/Restaurant/Healthcare/White Label/Industry) remain fully wired and one compile-time flag from returning to school builds
- **Platform:** all · **Module:** verticals / scope hygiene · **Role:** owner / release engineering
- **Evidence:** Verified hidden today: lib/core/config/school_build_scope.dart:19 (enabled=true), 22-41 (modules hidden), 54-84 (routes blocked) and route_guards.dart:411. But the destinations, routes and permission entries are all still live code: admin_navigation_provider.dart:165-220 declares Salon/Restaurant/Healthcare/Accommodation/White Label/Industry nav tiles; route_names.dart:172-214 declares their route trees; app_router.dart:1060-1115 registers them.
- **Impact:** No user-visible defect in the school build (this check PASSES), but the entire out-of-scope surface is retained behind a single boolean. Any build with enabled=false, or a future edit to the hidden-set, silently re-adds a salon to a school ERP — and the catalog inflates the mental model for every developer and QA pass.
- **Recommendation:** Per the owner's out-of-scope decision, delete (not hide) the vertical modules, or split them into a separate build target so a school release physically cannot contain them.
